They are named in `docs/HANDOFF.md`'s skip section and here, because the map records **pages** and a php binary is not a page. The lookup is gated on `APP === 'v4'` and `skip-list:check` now **pins** that gate (removing it gives exit 1 with the finding named; restoring it is green at 111 keys / 43 specs / 155 titles / 3 unresolvable sites), because with the map populated an ungated lookup would have skipped, in every v3 run, **22 tests that render at least one baseline** — the flip gate silently not comparing them. **A dead boot costs a worker's remaining tail (D84):** one `Address already in use` took four tests, `add.spec.ts` and three consecutive `visual-features.spec.ts` ones, so boot collisions block the flip **ahead of** D75 and D76 — the fix is a port per boot, not a retry — and a boot death also **hides** keys, since `/item/1/edit` was invisible for four runs only because each of them lost that test before it reached its page. **`qa-v2.spec.ts:56` is fixed at its cause** (D69, resolved 21:58): the add is a POST + 303 and the spec navigated away on the next line, so under load the request was aborted before `Empty drawer` existed. **The collision has a name (D85, 22:05).** `startV4` already takes the app port from `freePort()`, so the socket that raced was the **inspector**: one `--inspector-port 0` per boot, and `wrangler`'s own `validatePort` accepts 0, so the argv is legal. That reorders the end of G1 without changing its destination: the **boot fix comes before the promotion**, because a boot-clean run is what makes a key set trustworthy — `/item/1/edit` is a measured 501 that none of the 111 keys at `a1f4fa82` names, so the promotion re-bases the map **upward**. **The boot fix holds, and one listed key was a phantom (D86, 22:14).** The boot-clean harvest `45691829738` at `f28b0d32` reports **170 tests / expected 2 / skipped 165 / unexpected 3 / flaky 0** with no `wrangler dev exited` anywhere in the report, and proposes exactly **one** key, `v4 answers 501 for /item/1/edit` — D85’s prediction confirmed in the direction it was written: what a dead boot hides, a clean boot names. Its 165 skips are 100 + 10 + 55, so **110 of 111 keys applied**, and the eleventh — `originals.spec.ts`’s `settings`-fixture key — is **inert**, because that spec skips wholesale for `php` and its map fixture never runs. The map therefore counts **effective** keys, the promotion drops the inert one and adds `/item/1/edit`, and **D87** keeps the word “boot-clean” honest: five tests booted v4 in that run, so the fix rests on the seam’s four-boot measurement and its pin, and is re-tested when the map is small enough for boots to contend. **Landed since, at 23:09 (corrected from “Next” at 23:20):** the promotion (`75d5a3e9`, `0e06f49e` — 112 keys, `--write-handoff`, the inert `originals` key dropped, `/item/1/edit` and `/new` added), the `v4-booted` census D81 owed (`66003410`; `45697085355` reads 115 booted / 0 dead / 55 that never asked for a page), and the flip itself (`ce7fc637` at `68d3401c`), whose own run `45701796669` executed **3** tests, skipped 167 (112 under the map) and compared **2** baselines. What remains of G1 is the 507-file re-baseline and the port itself.

**The harvest is green, the census is measured, and the map is 112 effective keys (D88, 22:45).** Run `45697085355` at `0acfbc26`, read from artifact `15060833815`: **170 tests, expected 3 / skipped 167 / unexpected 0 / flaky 0**, **115 `v4-booted`** annotations and 167 `skip` annotations, with an empty proposed map. The 167 skips are **102** “v4 answers 501 for …” + **10** “the `sql` fixture has no v4 equivalent” + **55** “the spec shells `php` itself” (33 `autofill-suggest`, 12 `autofill-on-demand`, 8 `autofill-settings`, 2 `originals`), and the booted count is exactly 170 − 55: **every test that gets a live app records it and no test that ran lacks the record** (D81). **The map is 112 keys** — 102 by a 501 and 10 by the `sql` fixture, each stamped with the run that measured it (105 × `45671253354`, 5 × `45677452961`, 1 × `45691829738`, 1 × `45694324988`) — and since 102 + 10 = 112 keys produced 102 + 10 = 112 skips, **there is no listed key that cannot fire** (D86, now proven by a run). Read here at the head, not taken from the builder: `node v4/scripts/check-visual-skip-list.mjs` exits 0 with *“31 self-test cases, all as intended”* and *“43 spec(s), 155 test title(s); CI4_UNPORTED has 112 key(s), each naming a title that exists and a page docs/HANDOFF.md lists; 3 unresolvable title site(s) recorded”* — so the hand-off section and the map agree, generated rather than typed (D79, D80). **D75 is closed by a v4 fix**: `visual.spec.ts` “error page” passes against the untouched baseline, where it differed by 14,730 px at 22:14; `settings.consent` had been hardcoded `false`, so every page drew the cookie notice, and `aa60a4ea` ports v3’s `CookieConsent::request()` (`apotheke_consent=v1`; `requiredFrom()` false only for exactly `off`). One fidelity note with no action today: v3 keeps `ACCEPTED` as an array (`['v1']`) and v4 compares against one constant, so a later banner version (`v2`) is a code change in v4, not a config row. **D76 is closed by a carrier**: `fixtures.ts` watches `page.request` and the context’s own `request`, so an `APIRequestContext` 501 is recorded — `qa-fixes.spec.ts` is a key now. **What blocked the flip was not a red but the 102 pages that answer 501** (corrected at 23:20, after the flip landed): the map named them, and the flip at `ce7fc637` compares the **two** baselines whose pages v4 serves — not 512 — at `AE > 5,000` with the rest named and no silent skip. The 512-baseline comparison arrives with phase 2’s ports. **Gate coverage, recorded because it hid a defect for one commit:** `visual-boot:check` boots workerd and runs only in `v4.yml`’s workerd-smoke step (`v4.yml:127`), so the six local gates cannot catch a regression in it — `45694333366` did, one commit after a local green, on a 400-character slice of `reset: [` that `82f9d2db` widened to 1600 and `23009fd9` replaced with a bracket-matched block (715 characters, php seed at 620), so the width stopped being a clock.
### Phase 2 — Read-only catalog, HTML and API (3–4 pw)

Item list with every filter/sort/page (`ListFilters::DEFS`, `ItemList::search`), item detail, search, taxonomy pages,
labels/QR rendering, media GET (`/media/full|thumb`) from R2, and the catalog API (`/api/v1/items`, `/items/{id}`,
locations, categories, tags) with `openapi.yaml` + `docs/api.md` in the same PR.

**Gate G2:** golden pages byte-identical for list/detail/search/taxonomy; `visual.spec.ts` green; the API's captured answers
byte-identical — `v4/test/fixtures/php-api.json` replayed for the session routes, and the capture extended in the same PR that adds
a catalog route — because `tests/ApiContract.php` retired with the PHP suite.

### Phase 3 — Catalog writes (5–6 pw)

Item create/edit/delete through the same validation and history path, photos metadata, taxonomy writes, notes and
markdown, tags, favourites, lending, stocktake, bulk actions and merge, undo tokens, activity feed.

**Gate G3:** full functional Playwright suite green against v4; write-replay test — the same recorded write sequence
on D1, on local SQLite **and** on rqlite.

### Phase 4 — Media and autofill (3–4 pw, plus the D2 spike)

Upload, rotate, crop, reorder, cover flag, originals, thumbnails, HEIC path per D2; autofill client, cached `/v1/info`,
analysis copy generation, token policy.

**Gate G4:** photo round trip (upload → thumbnail → download → backup → restore) identical on PHP and v4;
autofill visual specs green; no upload path exceeds the Worker memory ceiling in the test matrix.

### Phase 5 — Backup, restore, import, export, jobs (4–6 pw)

ZIP writer/reader (fflate, streamed), restore with the existing chunked upload API, restore fence and sweep, CSV
import and export, `jobs` table replaced by DO alarms with the same observable behaviour (`/api/v1/jobs`).

**Gate G5:** backup round trip PHP → v4 → PHP byte-comparable in content; restore of a real v3.72 backup on v4 and of
a v4 backup on the PHP image; import/export specs green; jobs run from alarms and survive a restart.

### Phase 6 — Ops, self-host on workerd, packaging (3–4 pw)

`Ops` entrypoint calls object methods directly (no `ops.internal` signature); lifecycle export built in the object;
workerd distribution (capnp config, multi-stage Docker image, `/data` volume, port 9091, secrets from the
environment); the two compose files, `.env.example` and `docs/self-host-docker.md` per §11; fold `v4/src` into `edge/src` and delete the container code paths.

**Gate G6:** lifecycle tests green without a container; `scripts/selfhost-smoke.sh` passes on **both** compose files
(`docker-compose.yml` with rqlite, `docker-compose.sqlite.yml` single process) — `/healthz`, first-admin claim, create an
item, upload a file, take a backup, restore it, restart the stack and find the same catalogue (`/data` still there);
`docs/self-host-docker.md` followed literally by someone other than its author; `docs/self-host-workerd.md` written and followed literally by someone other than its author, covering
both database options.

### Phase 7 — Parity gate and merge decision (1–2 pw)

Full matrix: every Playwright project against v4 with untouched baselines; parity recorder zero unexplained diffs;
contract tests; performance sanity (list/detail p95 within 1.5× of PHP on the same data, **plus the statement
budget measured on v4's `Sql` wrapper and declared at measured + margin** — revision 50: p95 states the cost in
milliseconds, the budget states it in queries, and a page that regresses as N+1 fails faster and more legibly on
the second, especially on rqlite where every query is an HTTP round trip); upgrade notes; a merge
proposal with the diff summary.

**Gate G7:** E2E 100% green on `experimental-v4` with unmodified baselines, and Stathis says go. Merging `main` ←
`experimental-v4` is the release decision, not a build decision.

### Phase 8 — Decommission (post-merge, only with explicit approval)

Delete `ApothekeContainer` with a `deleted_classes` migration (destroys that object's storage — owner approval);
retire the container image, the `d1.internal` bridge and the Postgres wiring in the self-host docs; delete the PHP
application from the branch once v4 is `main`. **The PHP app keeps its Postgres support on `main` for v3 only; v4 never
speaks Postgres** — an operator coming from v3 Postgres moves to rqlite by export and import (§10.2), because rqlite,
not Postgres, is the self-host database.

Total: about 29–35 pw of build work (dropping Postgres removed the dialect layer; the rqlite driver, the D1 driver and provisioning, and the transaction audit add about 4 pw).

## 6. Test strategy (the gate machinery)

| Suite | Runs against | Proves |
|---|---|---|
| `v4/test` vitest (pool: workers) | real workerd, DO SQLite, R2 | unit and handler behaviour |
| Schema parity | PHP runner vs TS runner, empty + v3.72 dump, **on D1, local SQLite and rqlite** | database compatibility both ways |
| rqlite matrix (real `rqlited` in the job) | the TS driver against a real server | the self-host path does not rot |
| D1 matrix (emulated binding) | the D1 driver and its `batch()` unit of work | the hosted path does not rot |
| sqlite matrix (`DB_DRIVER=sqlite`, DO storage) | the same suite on the single-process driver, `test/sqlite.spec.ts`, and the database the DO wrote | the small self-host default does not rot |
| Golden pages | PHP-produced HTML corpus | DOM/route/text equality |
| API contract (`v4/test/fixtures/php-api.json`, 28 captured answers) | replay against v4 | `/api/v1` unchanged; bodies validated against `openapi.yaml` |
| Parity recorder (`tests/parity/recorder.php`, third target) | PHP app and v4 on the same steps | response equality with the existing allowlist quirks |
| Write replay | PHP and v4 from the same seed, on **all three** backends | identical table dumps and blob lists after writes |
| Playwright functional (`--grep-invert visual`) | v4 | every user flow |
| Playwright visual (`tests/visual/*.spec.ts`, 8 projects) | v4 | pixel equality with existing baselines |
| Backup round trip | v4 ↔ PHP image | format compatibility both directions |
| workerd self-host smoke | the built image, SQLite volume and a rqlite node | the self-host promise on both engines |

Rule: a phase gate is only signed off with a link to the CI run. "It works locally" is not a gate.

**Authorship of the branch of record (revision 96).** At `f7dc02da`, `git log origin/experimental-v4 --format=%ae | sort | uniq -c` counts **42 commits under five `pm@` addresses** — `pm@webapp.gr` 28, `pm@apotheke.local` 9, `pm@apotheke.invalid` 3, `pm@example.invalid` 1, `pm@web.gr` 1 — the oldest `a6b681ad`, revision 19, **2026-11-24T05:31:14+02:00**. That is one author under five identities, not five writers: the builder’s box carries `thordin9 <thordin9@gmail.com>` and no `pm@` identity in any config, and it does not touch this file. Nothing about a commit’s content follows from its author address; what follows is that an append-only revision stack cannot name its own author until one identity is used, which is a bookkeeping fix and not a gate. **Corrected 02:18 EET 2026-11-26 — the cause is known and it is mine:** every commit this box makes to this file runs through a push script that passes `git -c user.email=…` explicitly, so the address records **which script generation wrote the commit**, not which machine. `pm@webapp.gr` is the flag in `pushrev64.sh`, `pushrev68.sh` and `pushrev102.sh`, `pm@apotheke.local` the flag in `pushrev59.sh`, `pushrev61.sh` and `pushrev62.sh`; `pm@web.gr` (1 commit) matches no script on disk, so it came from an inline command or a script since deleted. Measured at `f7dab7f0`: **48 commits under those five addresses** — `pm@webapp.gr` 33, `pm@apotheke.local` 10, `pm@apotheke.invalid` 3, `pm@example.invalid` 1, `pm@web.gr` 1 — the growth from revision 96’s 42 being the commits since. Nothing about a commit’s content follows from this and no rebase is owed, but my “two writers will collide” was wrong twice over (D121)

**Pre-push rule (D30, revision 29).** The two rules are different in kind and both apply. Before any push to the branch of record, the changed surface must pass, locally: `tsc --noEmit`, `biome check .`, `depcruise src`, `routes:check`, `fixtures:check` and `vitest run`. The full suite was 74 s on the dedicated box until S1.4 and is **89.63 s** at `c8cf8d62` (103 s as the builder measured it; the replay adds 28 captured answers against a real DO, five of them bcrypt and throttle), and time was spent learning what a local run would have said: `2e38fad2` died on lint, `76dab1db` on a doubled `export`, `8608a057` on one 121-character line of formatting. A red push is now a choice rather than a circumstance - and a run whose lint step fails skips `routes:check`, `assets:check`, `fixtures:check` and `npm test`, so the steps that would have caught a real defect never execute.

**The seventh gate that is not a pre-push gate (D89).** `v4/scripts/check-visual-boot.mjs` boots workerd through the harness seam and runs only in CI — `v4.yml:127`, the `workerd smoke` job's step 6 — so the six gates above cannot see a regression in it, and that is not hypothetical: `45694333366` was red at `aa60a4ea` because a self-test read a 400-character slice of the harness and the D81 census annotation pushed the php seed past its end. A green local run is silent about this script by construction, so “six gates green” is a claim about six gates and never about this one.

**The flip's comparator rule (D90, D93).** A screenshot has changed when **more than 5,000 pixels differ**, counted with `threshold: 0` — Playwright's `maxDiffPixels: 5_000` in place of `maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.002`, a count rather than a ratio because a ratio’s budget is a different number on every viewport: 0.2% is **363 px on the iPhone SE, 517 on iPhone 13 and 691 on Pixel 7** — below that floor on **two of the three** phone projects — the Pixel 7’s 691 px sits just above the 663 px floor, which is why the count rule is the fix and not a tighter ratio — and **2,201 px on desktop**, where the ratio was *looser* than the count that replaced it (Playwright’s own device table; the sentence said “500 px” until `0ffd7490` corrected it, D95). The floor and the signal it is compared with are measured: worst noise **663 px** over two unplanted renders, smallest material change **46,411 px**. Consequences a reader must carry: the rule is **measured at `98c94dbb` on `v4/g1-map` and is in this branch's config since `68d3401c`**, whose own flip run reproduces the measurement (`45701796669`: 3 executed, 167 skipped, 112 boots, both baselines red at 6,000 with base 0) and whose landed successor repeats it (**`45705344087`**, job `139525758567`: the same shape line, the same two compared baselines, all four control lines at 6,000 pixels with base 0 — read from that run, D100); and under it a **one-pixel plant is green by construction**, so the control plants 6,000 (budget + 1,000) and asserts the reported count exceeds the budget — the locating property is the library self-test that plants 250 and reads 250 back. What this does not do: make the 29 nondeterministic files deterministic (D64's noise is measured, not removed), or re-baseline the 507 stale files, which are v3's own render and not v4's.

**The gate that cannot load is not a gate (D91).** A workflow file Actions cannot parse is invisible to every local check, and
`45699752450` at `d7fe8363` is the shape to recognise: created and failed in the same second (20:56:51Z) with **no jobs at all**, and a run
name that is the workflow file's path. A plain YAML scalar containing `: ` starts a mapping, so a step name with a colon in it took the whole
file out; `workflows:check` reads workflow text for PHP steps and never loads YAML, so all six gates were green on a file that could not
run. Every `.github/workflows/*.yml` is loaded by that check **before any trigger is read** — gating or not, because a file that does not load runs no job and so gates nothing — and the order is **closed at `0ffd7490`**, run `45705344110`. Measured by running it: the real tree prints **11 workflow file(s) load as YAML; 4 can gate `experimental-v4` (ci.yml, snapshots.yml, v4.yml, visual-v4.yml); no PHP step in any** and exits 0; a copy of the directory carrying `- name: Harvest: the map` exits **1** naming the file, the line and the message; an empty directory exits **1** rather than pass (**D98**). The class is D89’s either way: a gate that is not in the list is not a gate, and a gate that read no file proves nothing.

**A development copy may trail the branch of record only where the harvest does not read (D99).** `v4/g1-map` sits at `68d3401c` and the head is `0ffd7490`: **seven files apart** — `docs/HANDOFF.md` (5), `docs/v4-migration.md` (28), `playwright.config.ts` (4, **a comment**), `v4/docs/testing.md` (6), `package.json` + `package-lock.json` (a dev dependency), `v4/scripts/check-workflows-php-free.mjs` (72) — and none of them is a spec, a fixture, the map or `v4/src`. Pushing to that branch costs one 28–40 minute harvest, which is why it is allowed to trail; the bound is that the first commit touching `tests/visual/**`, `tests/visual/fixtures.ts`, `ci4-unported.json`, the comparator’s behaviour or `v4/src` recreates it at the head **before** the next map is taken.

**The sqlite leg (D31, revision 31).** `v4.yml`'s `sqlite` job names the driver instead of inheriting it (`DB_DRIVER=sqlite` carried into the test bindings by `vitest.config.ts`), runs the whole suite, fails unless the only pending assertions are the six `rqlite-live` specs *and* all six are present as `skipped` *and* `test/sqlite.spec.ts`'s six have run (`v4.yml:379`; five until `74868d8f`), then boots workerd on that driver (`/healthz` and `/readyz` reporting `sqlite`, schema `0036`, seed `done`) and reads the DO's database with the `sqlite3` CLI. What it proves: the single-process self-host default does not rot, and its contract is asserted by name. What it does not: workerd's SQLite is not a self-hoster's `node:sqlite` or system `sqlite3`, so nothing here says two processes may open that file at once — the app never opens it and has exactly one writer. The E2E runner
gets one new switch: `APOTHEKE_BASE_URL`, so the same suite points at PHP or v4 without edits.

## 7. Risks

| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Image pipeline (GD/Imagick, HEIC, 40 MP) cannot fit 128 MB | Uploads regress, self-host loses HEIC | D2 decision + phase 4 spike; browser-side pre-resize; adapter isolates the choice |
| bcrypt CPU cost on many sign-ins | slow sign-in, CPU billing | keep cost 12, measure; `limits.cpu_ms` raised on hosted; no rehash migration |
| FTS5 or alarms missing/experimental on local workerd | search or jobs differ self-hosted | phase 0 spikes; fallback `like` path already exists in `SearchIndex`; alarm sweep on next request |
| libsodium secretbox incompatibility | settings with secrets unreadable | fixture test with a PHP-produced value in phase 0 |
| A write path cannot be expressed as one rqlite batch (no transaction across requests) | the save/import paths get restructured late | transaction audit in S0.4 classifies all 10 call sites before phase 1; dependent steps move into SQL (`last_insert_rowid()` inside the batch) or the case comes back to Stathis |
| The rqlite path rots because everyone develops on the DO | self-host breaks surface late, after hosted is green, and the self-host default is the one nobody runs | the `v4.yml` rqlite job runs from S0.8 on every PR against a real `rqlited`, repeating migration, parity and handler suites; G0/G3/G6 run on all three backends; no slice is accepted with rqlite red |
| View porting drift (thousands of DOM details) | visual suite fails late and expensively | golden corpus per page in phase 2, visual per phase, no baseline updates without sign-off |
| ZIP format drift | backups unreadable by 2.6.x/PHP | two-direction round trip in gate G5 |
| Long-lived branch diverges from main | painful merge | `main` frozen by rule, so divergence is bounded; weekly rebase of the branch onto `main` is forbidden — only forward merges of approved security fixes |
| workerd features still experimental (localDisk, bucket, r2.js) | self-host fragility | pin workerd version; document the pin; smoke test on every PR |
| Scope creep into an SPA rewrite | doubles the work | D3: v4 keeps the server-rendered UI; the SPA stays a separate track |

## 8. Decisions (answered 2026-11-23, 12:52–12:59 EET)

| # | Decision | Answer | Effect on the plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Database backends | **Revised 12:58 — Postgres dropped.** Hosted defaults to **D1**; self-host defaults to **rqlite**, plain SQLite supported; no migration path, v3 export → v4 import | Plan §10, S0.7 (D1) and S0.8 (rqlite), all three backends in G0/G3/G6 |
| D9 | Branch protection on `main` and `experimental-v4` | **No** — neither branch is protected (13:17). The `main-freeze` workflow is the only gate, and it only sees pull requests | Nothing to configure; the freeze is discipline. Direct pushes to `main` are possible and would not be caught by CI |
| D8 | Hosted D1 tenancy | **Answered 12:59 — one D1 database per installation**, no shared database. No data move from current hosted installs: they start fresh and re-import a backup if needed | Control plane stores the D1 database id; provisioning and deletion run through the existing lifecycle jobs |
| D2 | Media pipeline | **Yes as recommended**: server work capped at 12 MP, big phone photos pre-resized in the browser, HEIC decoded client-side, originals stored untouched | Phase 4 as planned; non-browser uploads over 12 MP get a clear 422 |
| D3 | UI through the port | **Server-rendered HTML stays for now**; the SPA remains a separate track | Phase 2 ports views, not a client app |
| D4 | Evidence before `main` ← `experimental-v4` | **G7 only** — every suite green on the branch with untouched baselines, then Stathis's go. No dress rehearsal, no shadow period | Phase 7 is G7 + the merge proposal: 1–2 pw, nothing extra |
| D5 | Sessions at cutover | **One re-login per installation** is acceptable | PHP session serialisation is not ported |
| D6 | Workers Paid and CPU budget | **Acceptable** | bcrypt cost 12 and image work stay; cost re-checked at phase 4 |
| D7 | Switch-over | **Ping Stathis when the branch work is complete and ready to switch**; he makes the go call | G7 ends with a written merge proposal, never a merge |
| D10 | Write retries on rqlite after a `503` | **Answered 13:40 — every write unit carries an idempotency key** (`_write_log`, UUID per unit of work, conflict mapped to success). Reads retry freely; connect failures retry without a key; nothing after bytes leave the socket does | Rule 10 in §10; one table and one index in the schema, swept by the alarm job; same envelope on D1 so call sites are written once |
| D11 | Hosted refusals for inline taxonomy (`422`) and photo duplication (`501`) | **Answered 21:15 — both are lifted in v4.** They exist only because the pre-batch code could not return a `Ref` from taxonomy create and `PhotoRepository::insert()` took `int`; the batch path removes the constraint, so re-shipping the refusal would port a limitation we just deleted | Audit sites #7 (`ItemController::save()`) and #8 (`Duplicate::copy()`) are rewritten in phase 1; `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and `docs/api.md` change in the same PR as the code. Revisit only if returning a `Ref` drags permission/auth paths into the rewrite, or if an external consumer of the documented `422`/`501` is found |
| D12 | What is left of the `Installation` DO once it stops holding the database | **Answered 21:00 — keep one per installation, demoted** from database owner to scheduler + lock: `alarms` for per-installation jobs (Cron Triggers are per-Worker, not per-installation), `blockConcurrencyWhile` as the migration lock and refuse-if-newer gate, lease/cursor state only. No application data, off the request path. **Exception:** `DB_DRIVER=sqlite` still uses `ctx.storage.sql`, so the DO owns the database in exactly that one mode | S0.3; hosted D1 and rqlite both run the reduced DO, and no handler may know which mode it is in (the async `Sql` adapter's job). §10.1 updated |
| D13 | What "identical schema" means now that v4 adds `_write_log` | **Answered 23:22 — a literal allowlist, checked in both directions.** The PHP runner's oracle is 64 objects (36 tables, 28 indexes) and v4 adds exactly `_write_log` and `idx_write_log_created`, both inside 0036 in the migration chain. G0 asserts TS ⊇ PHP object-for-object **and** TS minus that literal pair = PHP exactly | G0 amended in §5; the parity test compares a literal set pair, so a new object on either side fails CI instead of silently widening the allowlist |
| D14 | First boot: seeding, and what readiness means before it finishes | **Answered 23:22 — a seed gate.** 64 settings, 4 roles and the first user are seeded inside `blockConcurrencyWhile` before the first request is served, and marked by a seed row rather than inferred from "the tables look empty"; `/readyz` answers **503 while a seed is pending**, `/healthz` answers **200 always** | S0.3; the PHP fixture's `seed_rows` (64 settings, 4 roles, 1 user) are compared on D1 and on DO SQLite, and a second seed is idempotent. The compose healthcheck probes `/healthz`, so an unseeded container is never restarted mid-seed |
| D15 | How `enc:v1:` secrets are sealed once `libsodium-wrappers` cannot run in workerd | **Answered 23:44 — write XSalsa20-Poly1305 by hand in `src/platform/secretbox.ts`.** workerd disables WebAssembly codegen, so the library does not load at all — this is not a packaging preference, it is a hard runtime limit. The format is libsodium's own secretbox, so existing `enc:v1:` values keep working and no user re-enters a secret | §3's crypto row; the interop question was closed by sealing and opening values in both directions against real `sodium_crypto_secretbox` (PHP 8.4.23), which is the only evidence that counts. Recorded as a numbered decision because it changes what every secret at rest depends on |
| D16 | Where v4's compose files and `.env.example` live before the cutover | **Answered 03:30 — under `v4/`.** The repo root already ships v3's `docker-compose.yml` (`d631dc53`, the file every current self-hoster runs) and v3's `.env.example` (`7c3da402`), and `docs/getting-started.md` / `docs/deployment-and-operations.md` describe them. Overwriting them in phase 0 would replace a working v3 stack with a v4 one that has no router, no auth and no pages yet | S0.9 writes `v4/docker-compose.yml`, `v4/docker-compose.sqlite.yml`, `v4/.env.example`, and `scripts/selfhost-smoke.sh` takes the compose path as an argument. P7 moves them to the root in one PR, keeps v3's file for one release as `docker-compose.v3.yml`, and rewrites the two v3 docs in the same PR |
| D17 | How rqlite's storage is mounted, once §11's two-volume row proved impossible | **Answered 03:40 — one volume at `/rqlite/file`.** rqlite v10.7.4 keeps the raft log and the SQLite database inside one data directory, and the released binary has no `-raft-dir`-style flag to split them; the image pre-creates `/rqlite/file` owned by uid 1000, so mounting an empty named volume elsewhere leaves it root-owned and unwritable. The stack therefore mounts `rqlite-data:/rqlite/file` with `DATA_DIR=/rqlite/file/data` | S0.9 shipped this and reported it rather than editing the plan; §10.3 and §11's service contract are corrected here. The advertised addresses stay pinned to the service name (`HTTP_ADV_ADDR`/`RAFT_ADV_ADDR`), because a single-node cluster that re-advertises a new hostname after `docker compose up --force-recreate` can refuse its own old address |
| D18 | The two PHP host/CORS behaviours whose variables are not in the sixteen-name contract | **Answered 04:15 — they stay out of the contract for phase 0, and the narrowing is documented rather than silent.** `ALLOWED_HOSTS` is not needed to serve correctly: `hostcheck` accepts IP literals, `localhost`, `*.localhost` and `PUBLIC_URL`'s own host. `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` is empty, which is v3's own self-host default and the safe direction — no cross-origin is allowed rather than all | S0.5; `v4/docs/router.md` names both as not ported, with the consequence. Revisit only if a self-hoster needs either: adding one is a change to §11's sixteen names, which then also changes `.env.example`, both compose files and the walkthrough in one commit, and is not a code-only edit |
| D19 | When S0.5's 401 scaffold for page routes retires | **Answered 05:20 — not in S1.2.** `/login` is the only ported page and it needs no session, so restoring v3's 303 redirect would change a behaviour nothing can observe yet; the 401 stays named as scaffold until the first ported page that needs a session, which is P2 | S1.2 dispatches without it. The slice that lands a session-gated page flips that route's class and asserts the list the way `unported.spec.ts` does, rather than flipping it in a slice that cannot show the difference |
| D20 | S1.2's session cookie name and flags, and whether a session may exist without consent | **Answered at 05:31 in revision 19 — v3 wins on both, and the plan was wrong.** Read from v3 at `main@4a187d0f`: `SessionGate.php` has `COOKIE='apothekesid'` and `SECURE_COOKIE='__Host-apothekesid'`, chosen by `cookieName(bool $secure)`; `RequestScheme::secureCookie()` derives that bit; `SessionCookie.php` sets `lifetime` = session lifetime in seconds, `path=/`, `httponly`, `samesite=Lax`, no domain — and returns before any session is loaded or started when consent is absent and no session cookie is carried | §4.4 and §5's S1.2 bullet are corrected in this revision, not left to the implementer. S1.2 implements v3's name and flags and states in its report where the consent gate lands — in the slice, or deferred with a named scaffold and the slice that retires it. A sign-in that issues a session cookie to a browser with no consent answer is a behaviour change and must not ship unnamed |
| D21 | S1.2's sign-in throttle: per-session or per-account, and does the 429 carry `Retry-After` | **Answered 05:52 — per address + account, exactly as v3 counts it, and the header is a named addition.** The DoD sentence in revision 20 ("the same N from a fresh session are not") described a session-keyed counter, which is the bypass the limit exists to close: `RateLimiter::LIMITS` is `login` 10/900 s keyed by `sha256(address "\0" account key)` plus `login_address` 30/900 s keyed by address alone, read from `main@4a187d0f`. | The builder implemented v3 and said the plan was wrong; the plan is corrected above rather than the code. The 429's `Retry-After` stays because I asked for it, and it is labelled a divergence from v3 in §5 so a later parity test does not read it as a regression. What the slice still owes on this point: where the counter lives in v4 (`auth_attempts`), and what a sign-in answers when that store is unavailable — v3 fails the request through `Writes::check`, it does not fail open |
| D22 | S1.2's last bullet, `/claim`, when the other eleven are green | **Answered 07:31 — split, not dropped, and not folded forward.** S1.2 closes on what it built and proved; the claim is **S1.2b**, the next slice, before S1.3 | The alternative — folding it into S1.3 — hides a slice's own DoD bullet inside another slice's, which is exactly the quiet omission a DoD exists to prevent. S1.2b starts only after its report names where the claim token lives, because v3 reads it from a store this port has not built and that is a §4/§11 choice |
| D23 | Two v3 consent knobs have no v4 equivalent: `COOKIE_CONSENT=off` and `COOKIE_DOMAIN` | **Answered 07:31 — not ported, and recorded rather than left as an unstated gap.** Neither is among the sixteen names (§11), and the sixteen stays sixteen (D18 is the precedent) | Consequences, stated plainly so nobody discovers them at a deploy: v4 always requires consent, so an operator who ran v3 with the notice off now gets the notice once per browser; and the consent cookie is always host-only, so an operator who shared consent across subdomains re-answers it per host. Both fail in the safe direction — more consent, not less. Revisit only if an installation asks: that is a §11 change and a plan revision, not a code change |
| D24 | The one environment name `/claim` needs that v4 does not carry (`HOSTED_CLAIM_HOURS`), and whether the claim is blocked on it | **Answered 07:41 — not ported; v4 uses v3's default of 72 hours with v3's 1–720 clamp as a constant.** The premise that two names were missing is wrong: `APOTHEKE_HOSTED` is already in §11's contract (`src/env.ts:17`, `src/platform/config.ts:81`, `wrangler.jsonc:60`, `.env.example:66`), so the hosted gate for `/claim` needs no plan change. Only the window knob is v3's alone (`ApothekeConfig.php:61`, default at `:266`, clamp at `:118`) | Consequence, stated rather than discovered at a deploy: a hosted install that ran v3 with `HOSTED_CLAIM_HOURS=24` gets 72 hours in v4 — the longer window for a first-admin token is the less safe direction, which is why it is written down. Revisit if our own hosting needs a different window: that is a §11 change (`.env.example`, both compose files, the walkthrough, one commit) and a plan revision, not a code-only edit |
| D25 | Who mints a claim token at all: a slice, or the operator console | **Answered 10:13 — C1 owns it, and the consequence is written down rather than discovered at G1.** `createClaim()` exists and is tested; nothing in the product calls it. v3 issues the link from a spark command (`ClaimCreateCommand`), and v4's operator console is C1. Inventing a CLI now would invent product surface for a one-off operator action, in the phase whose whole point is parity before addition. | The consequence, stated so nobody finds it at the gate: **`/claim` is unreachable by an operator until C1**, so **G1's "claim the first admin" step cannot be demonstrated end to end** before then. If G1 arrives first, the smallest honest fix is one documented one-shot invocation recorded as S1.2c — not a CLI framework. Nothing else in S1.2b is blocked by this decision |
| D26 | `StatementResult.changes` reports `0` on one of the three backends | **Answered 10:13 — do not change the driver; make the type say "unreported".** `DurableObjectSql.batch()` cannot report per-statement effects, because its unit of work is the DO's `transactionSync`: D1 reports `meta.changes`, rqlite `rows_affected`, and the single-process backend returns `{changes: 0, lastRowId: null}` for every statement on purpose. No product code reads `.changes` — the only reader is `test/rqlite-live.spec.ts:62`, which runs against rqlite, where the value is true. | Behaviour is decided **from state, never from a statement result**; that is what the slice's fix does, and it is the same rule as the `btoa` lesson — a value's contract is proven by reading it back, not by trusting what wrote it. `changes` becomes `number \| null` with `null` meaning *this backend does not report it*, so TypeScript refuses a read that assumes a count instead of a comment asking politely. That change and the `_write_log` test-noise quietening go in one hygiene commit of their own, not inside a slice. Revisit only if a caller genuinely needs counts, which would mean the DO's batch RPC returning per-statement `meta` — a storage-layer change with the session flush in its blast radius |
| D27 | The `RoleAdmin` last-manager race: fix it on `main` now, or inside v4 phase 1 | **Answered 12:49 by Stathis — the `EXISTS` assert inside v4 phase 1, and no freeze-line fix on `main`.** The race is real: two concurrent `updateLocked()`/`deleteLocked()` calls can each read a manager count of two and each remove one, leaving none, and nothing in the schema forbids it. The fix chosen is the assert S1.3's DoD already required — read the count and refuse the write **in the same unit** — so it costs no extra slice work and needs no release of its own | What it costs, stated rather than implied: **the gap stays open on hosted D1 until v4 phase 1 ships and that installation is cut over.** Its reach is narrower than it sounds: the sequential path is already guarded (`RoleAdmin::managers()` covers a single request), so what is open is two edits landing in the same write unit — a double submit or two operators at once. What it does not do: it does not touch `main`, so v3.72.0 stays the frozen artefact, no tag moves, and the freeze keeps its meaning |
| D28 | S1.3's increment 2: split by **page** or by **handler** | **Answered 13:36 — by handler.** 2a is the page port, `GET settings/security` and the three users POSTs; 2b is the three roles POSTs, the JSON branch and the refusal assertions | A page split looks tidier on paper and fails in practice: `SecurityView::page()` is **one** 279-line document — the users table (`:137-159`), the role summary (`:82`) and the roles editor (`:173-216`) are sections of it — so a page split ships a commit that cannot render and therefore cannot be green. The handler split keeps each commit shippable and reviewable at roughly 700-900 and 350-400 lines. What it costs: between 2a and 2b the roles forms POST into the 501 handler, so 2a's docs must say so rather than let an operator find it |
| D29 | Who owns making `sessioncsrf` real: increment 2a, its own commit, or a later slice | **Answered 14:10 — it lands inside 2a, and it is recorded here as a decision rather than a silent scope change.** The filter refused every unsafe method with a phase-0 403, so every users POST in 2a would have been unreachable: the increment could not be tested, let alone shipped, without it. It is now v3's `CsrfCheck` — **419**, `Your session expired. Reload the page and try again.` as the app's error page and `{"ok":false,"error":"Your session expired."}` for a fetch, from `main@4a187d0f` (`modules/Core/Http/CsrfCheck.php:18`, `:23`) — with the body parsed once before the chain (`src/http/form.ts`), because a filter cannot await. | It is a decision about **every form write**, not about this page, which is why it needs a number: the phase-0 stance "refuse what cannot be checked" ends for all of them, the `sessioncsrf` entry leaves the deferred list, and `filters.spec.ts` now asserts 419 where it asserted 403. Re-splitting it into its own commit was considered and rejected: it would mean reverting `65d63014` to re-land the same 41 lines in two pieces, buying a red window for an audit trail this row already provides. What it costs: a wrong token is now a v3-shaped 419 everywhere instead of a plain refusal, so from 2a on any form POST is only as safe as the token the rendered page carried — which is exactly v3's own contract (`SEC-N10`), not a new one |
| D30 | Where the pre-push gates run, now that the dev VM is dedicated to Apotheke | **Answered 18:10 - locally, and the full suite is one of them.** The rebuilt box ran `vitest run` in 74 s with CI's own counts (28 files passed / 1 skipped, 248 tests passed / 6 skipped at `5db8a9f8`), and the suite is **89.63 s** at `c8cf8d62` measured here (300 tests passed / 6 skipped) and **103 s** as the builder measured it, because S1.4's replay is part of it, where the old box wedged at load 24 and made CI the only test runner. **The gate list is now six commands, `vitest run` included, before every push**; CI stays the sign-off authority (section 6) because a local run is still not a link to a run. The cost is one deliberate wait per push; the benefit is that a failure stops at the machine that can explain it instead of at a job whose lint step skipped the four steps behind it. **Reach: developer discipline only** - no CI job changes, no new dependency, and the six rqlite-live specs stay declared-skipped in the unit job, so `numPendingTests == 0` in the rqlite job remains the guard that they ran there. **Amended in revision 35:** at `61ad78b5` the suite is **102.97 s** here and **121 s** as the builder measured it (32 files passed / 1 skipped, 314 tests passed / 6 skipped), and one of its specs is bcrypt-scale work that declares its own deadline (**D33**) — a runner slower than this box is exactly what the local gates cannot see. |
| D33 | Why one test passed here and failed on CI, and what a deliberately slow test owes the gate | **Answered 10:34 — a test whose work is bcrypt-scale declares its own deadline; 5 s is an assumption about the runner, not a budget.** S1.5's throttle replay makes eleven wrong current-password attempts, each a bcrypt verify at PHP 8.4's default cost, plus two sign-ins: ~4 s here, over vitest's 5 s default on CI's runners, so `8075c68e` went red in two jobs (`45576582842`: the unit job's `npm test`, and rqlite's runner step) while all six local gates were green, and `61ad78b5` gives that test `60_000` with the measurement in the comment — the same eleven requests, the same assertions, no behaviour change. The lesson is not "raise timeouts": a spec doing deliberately expensive work states its deadline once, and a green local run cannot rule out a runner slower than this box. CI stays the sign-off authority: `45577510845` green at that head. |
| D34 | Which specs must declare their own vitest deadline, now that the default has failed three times on CI in two hours | **Answered 10:48 — the heavy three kinds do; cheap tests keep the default so a real hang still fails fast.** In the class — **named, not described, since revision 40**: exactly the six specs that carry `SLOW_TEST_MS` (`v4/test/helpers/deadline.ts`, listed in `v4/docs/testing.md`) — `readiness` (`test/readiness.spec.ts:50` calls `prepareInstallation()` twice and timed out at 5069 ms on CI for a docs-only commit), `seed-parity`'s D1 seed (4683 ms), the `account` throttle replay (3601 ms), the `signin` throttle replay (5808 ms on the sqlite leg of `45579682071`, the eleven-attempt replay `61ad78b5` ported) the `probes` sweep, and `api-session`'s eleventh-attempt replay (ten wrong sign-ins, each a bcrypt verify and a window wait; timed out at **5078 ms** here — the local instance, recorded in `v4/docs/testing.md`, with no CI run of its own, since `ab1e8238` was pushed together with `25a1f052` and `45600021396` covers it as an ancestor). Considered and deliberately left on the 5 s default, with their measurements: `seed.spec.ts` 1221 ms, `schema-parity.spec.ts` 2123 ms for six, `migrations.spec.ts` 940 ms. Each carries its deadline next to the test with the measurement in the comment, so the number is not mistaken for slack. **A count in a planning document names its unit, the commit it describes and the command that produced it** — revision 55: “111 heads on origin” was written from no command at all; `git ls-remote --heads | wc -l` on `thordin9/apotheke` says 121. Out of the class, deliberately: everything else keeps 5 s. Reach: developer discipline plus one line per heavy spec — no global `testTimeout`, because a global bump hides the hang this rule exists to keep visible. Evidence: `45576582842` (S1.5's first landing, the throttle replay), `45578984620` (a documents-only commit, `readiness.spec.ts:50` at 5069 ms) and `45579682071` (documents only as well — the same throttle replay, 5808 ms, in the **sqlite job**). Re-read per attempt, because a cancelled re-run is not a green one: only `45578984620` has a green re-run; `45576582842` was superseded by `61ad78b5` (run `45577510845`) and `45579682071`'s attempt 2 was cancelled at `Run npm ci` by the push of `560270bc`, whose run `45580204667` is green. Counting, because I got it wrong once: there are **four red instances on CI across three specs** — the `account` replay failed in **both** the unit and rqlite jobs of `45576582842` — `ab1e8238`'s comment "has killed four times on CI" was accurate about instances, `v4/docs/testing.md`'s "three tests" was accurate about distinct specs, and the 5078 ms is the fifth instance and local. **Settled by revision 45, and given a unit by revision 48:** side by side those two sentences read as five against four, so `51b0a7e9` names the unit in each file rather than lowering either number — the comment now reads **four CI job runs of three specs (the account replay, once in each of two jobs of one run)**, and the doc says three tests, four red job runs, and a fourth found off CI. The rule: when two files state one fact at different granularities, name the unit in both; picking the smaller number deletes the evidence. |
| D35 | Whether a status code quoted in a PM brief is a spec the builder must implement or a claim it must check | **Answered 12:03 — a claim, checked against source and the captures, and two of mine were wrong.** The S1.4/S1.5 brief said `POST /api/v1/session` answers 201 and that the API's CSRF refusal is 419; v3 answers **200** with GET's body and **403 `csrf.invalid`** (`ApiCsrf.php:31`, the `$refused` assignment — `:34` is the `return $refused` in the SameOrigin branch), and 419 is the *page* forms' `sessioncsrf` (D29). The builder checked and landed both right | What it costs: nothing to run — this file never carried the wrong numbers, so no document is corrected. What it changes: a number in a brief now has the same standing as a claim in a doc, and the builder's counter-evidence wins on the spot; the standard I apply to its reports, applied backwards |
| D36 | Whether the v4 line keeps a PHP test system | **Retired — Stathis, 12:31 EET on 2026-11-25, choosing "A+B" of the three options put to him.** Three parts, in this order, because a proof is not deleted before its replacement exists: **(a)** PHP leaves the v4 gate — `fixtures:check` stays hash-only TypeScript (it already is) and regeneration moves behind an explicit `fixtures:capture`, documented as needing PHP 8.4 CLI with `intl`+`sqlite3`, never run in CI; **(b)** the **231** `.php` suite files retire from `experimental-v4` (`245` was every `.php` under `tests/`; the 14 that remain are ledgered under D37) — `tests/Unit` (82), `tests/Modules` (140), the 9 root helpers, `phpunit.xml`, `tests/phpunit-bootstrap.php`, `tests/Modules/phpunit.xml.dist` — with `ci.yml`'s 11 PHP-coupled jobs reduced to what still applies to a frozen v3; **(c)** the browser suite stops driving PHP — `tests/visual/fixtures.ts:148` boots v4 instead of `php -S` and seeds through v4's own seed instead of `tests/visual/ci4-fixtures.php`, with a typed map naming every spec whose page is still a 501. **What it must not do:** delete or regenerate a single one of the 512 baseline PNGs, delete a spec to make the harness green, or touch `main` — which keeps its copy until cutover so a `revert`/`security` hotfix still has a net | What it costs: one small increment for (a)+(b) plus G1's harness for (c); `ci.yml` drops from 12 jobs to the 2 that are language-agnostic. What it does not do: change any v4 gate's evidence, the seven oracles, or the fixtures' role as the record of what v3 answers | What it changes: v4's proof of itself becomes TypeScript end to end — vitest for behaviour, Playwright for pages — and PHP becomes a tool used to *capture* v3 rather than a system the branch runs |
| D37 | Whether the retired PHP corpus is deleted or replaced, after D36b said "delete" and Stathis said "rewrite any tests in typescript" | **Replaced — Stathis, 12:32 EET on 2026-11-25, correcting my brief; D36b's wording was the error, not the builder's execution.** Every one of the 231 retired paths gets exactly one destination: `ported` (a TypeScript spec, named), `scheduled` (the slice that builds the behaviour, so the test cannot precede the code), or `dropped` (v4 makes it meaningless, with a reason). The guard is `v4/test/php-ledger.json` plus `npm run ledger:check` in the unit job — 231 rows reconciled against the deleting commit, failing on a missing row, a dangling `ported` target, or a `scheduled` row naming a slice this plan does not have. Why not port everything today: of the 1,435 methods, Catalog 19, Backup 160, Autofill 37, Media 21 and ImportExport 19 describe endpoints that answer 501 — a test written against a 501 asserts the 501, and is worse than no test because it goes green. Why not leave the rest to memory: "we will test it when we build it" is the sentence that produced D33 and D34, and this line has now paid three times for a suite that was believed rather than checked | What it costs: the ledger plus S1.7's Auth and Core ports; and one ledger row owed by every later slice. **Extended by revision 43:** the ledger covers **245** rows, not 231 — the 231 retired suite paths plus the **14** `.php` that survive `5d65939e` under `tests/` (13 v3 tools and `tests/visual/ci4-fixtures.php`), each with a destination, because Stathis's words were *no php in the v4 branch*; the 6 `.php` under `v4/scripts/` are the capture generators D36a keeps (offline only, never in CI) and are named in the ledger as the deliberate exception. What it does not do: restore a `.php` file to this line, or change what `main` runs. **Extended by revision 44:** two destinations this row named were wrong — `Catalog`'s 19 are P2 9 / P3 3 / P4 7, not P2, and `Auth`'s 131 cannot all be ported or dropped today because 41 of them test routes that answer 501; the rule is now that a row scheduled to a slice must leave it ported or re-pointed, never still pointing at a slice that has closed. **Extended by revision 46:** the 14 survivors are **3 scheduled** (G1 ×2, P7 ×1) + **11 dropped**; `tests/parity/recorder.php` stays `scheduled` to P7 because `ParityRecorderTest` is scheduled there and the recorder is the spec for the TypeScript diff, and `ledger:check` now also fails on any committed `.php` it was never told about — verified green with a planted file at `25a1f052` and exit 1 at `56d7347a` |
| D38 | Whether `validName()`'s explicit NUL guard is reachable in v4, after a negative control failed to fail | **Answered 13:39 — it is unreachable, it stays, and the comment is what changes.** Disabling the guard left `test/auth/users.spec.ts` green: PHP's `trim()` strips `"\0"` and JavaScript's does not, so the character class refuses a NUL anyway — with the same `SHAPE` error, so nothing observable differs. Kept because it states the PHP rule directly and becomes load-bearing the day the class is loosened; corrected because its docblock implied a distinct rule v4 does not have. `validPassword()`'s NUL check is the opposite case — reachable and distinct (“Passwords cannot contain a null character.”), so it stays and stays asserted. Four sites carry the guard: `users.ts:56,82` and `first-admin-claim.ts:72,97` | A negative control that catches nothing is information, not a failure: it is the only way to learn a guard has no observable effect. No behaviour change, so no gate moves |
| D39 | A failed `roles` read: v3 answered built-in defaults, v4 fails loudly — which is right, and what happens at G7 | **Answered 13:39 — keep v4's behaviour and name it as an approved divergence.** v3 caught the database error and rendered the built-ins; v4 lets the read fail, because an install that has migrated cannot be “no table yet”, and masking an outage as defaults offers a roles editor that cannot write. It is pinned as a case at `test/auth/roles.spec.ts:86`, and it joins the recorder's four approved quirks (N-2, QA L-2, N-4, I-3) as something G7 must not read as a port bug | G7's differential gate will show this difference; without a row it would be triaged as a regression. One row, no code |
| D47 | Which increment owns a DoD clause that spans two increments (“no `php` process started”) | **Answered 17:26 — it splits, and each increment is jud **[This row’s tail was cut by the revision 61–63 truncation and restored here only as far as the surviving text goes, 2026-11-26; the §9 entry for revision 61 is the rest of the record.]** |
| D50 | How a revision header’s timestamp is produced | **Answered 17:27 — by the script that commits, from `date` at write time, never typed.** Revision 61’s header said 17:26 against a 17:25:36 commit, after revision 53 was 1m47s early and revision 54 carried a count no command had produced: three instances of one carrier, a human estimate inside a mechanical step. An estimate cannot be audited and a clock read can, so the stamp moved into the script that commits | One line of script discipline, and every later header **[This row’s tail was cut by the revision 61–63 truncation and restored here only as far as the surviving text goes, 2026-11-26; the §9 entry for revision 62 is the rest of the record.]** |
| D51 | Whether the one-pixel control can be run anywhere | **Answered 17:33 — only by the pinned `@playwright/test` 1.65.1, and only if its red names the planted pixel.** `package.json` pins 1.65.1; this box has no root `node_modules` and the only install is 1.60.0 from another repo, so a red from 1.60’s chromium against baselines rendered by 1.65.1 is a browser mismatch, not evidence about the app. A red is exactly what a negative control wants, which is why exit status alone cannot be its evidence: the run must name the spec and locate the diff at the planted pixel. So the dispatch-only instrument job lands first as the control’s harness, the control goes red inside it, then seeding through v4, then the default flip (**D48**) | G1’s order changes: the control is no longer runnable by hand here |
| D52 | How an edit to the durable sections is verified | **Answered 17:33 — by diff, never by assertion.** Revision 61 truncated six spans of the durable section while every check stayed green, because no CI step reads prose: the S1.7 clause lost its 33 rows (Core 31, `Layout` 1, `D1Bootstrap` 1) and clauses (3)(4)(5), the “what this slice does not do” paragraph and revision 50’s `RoundTripBudgetTest` note; “**Gate G1** is not a slice” vanished; and Gate G0’s opening became “`.cjs`.ds (one SQLite schema…”. An edit to §0–§11 now quotes the span it replaces, reads `git diff` of the doc in full before committing, and asserts the byte count both ways | Every durable edit, mine included; the repair is revision 63 |
| D53 | Whether the 512 baselines can gate the flip, after the instrument measured v4's login at 1,098,316 of 1,100,800 pixels | **Answered 17:52 — no; they are pre-token-change, and the re-baseline is its own slice, rendered by the app of record.** `1f86dd66` (2026-11-21) replaced the palette and re-rendered exactly five PNGs; the scan says 265 files still carry the old `#f5f5f7` and 5 carry the new `#fdfcfa`, so v3 cannot pass its own suite and D48's “zero diff” was unreachable by construction. Rendered **from v3 at a pinned commit, never from v4**, with four controls (five-file byte-identity, render determinism, a planted CSS change, and — **D55**, 18:03 — the fifteen sibling files must change, because the cause is a project-filtered re-render: `1f86dd66` re-rendered `desktop-chromium` only, 5 new against 15 stale inside one spec), rendered by `main`’s own `snapshots.yml` dispatched at `ref=main` so no commit touches `main`, in one PNG-only commit. A baseline may be re-rendered from the app of record and never from the app under test — the second half of the rule that forbids changing a baseline to pass. |
| D54 | Whether the instrument can be dispatch-only, as D51 said | **Answered 17:52 — not while `main` is frozen; it runs on a push to `v4/visual-instrument` only.** GitHub will not dispatch a workflow that does not exist on the default branch, and `main` sits at `4a187d0f`. So it gates nothing, runs nowhere else, and costs one run when a reading is wanted; `workflows:check` names it as not gating. It moves to `workflow_dispatch` at the cutover, and a dead branch is the cost of the deviation — named rather than hidden. |
ed on its half.** The boot seam delivered no `php -S` app server, asserted on the seam’s argv with a negative case; seeding still shells `ci4-fixtures.php`, so literal zero-php belongs to the seeding increment. My sentence asked one commit for evidence another commit produces, which is how a DoD becomes unfalsifiable | One clause split in Gate G1’s paragraph, and the check that asserts the seam’s argv |
| D48 | Whether v4 becomes the visual harness’s default now or at G1’s end | **Answered 17:26 — opt-in until the map exists; the default flips as G1’s last step, gated by 512 unchanged baselines against a v4-booted run.** **Corrected 23:20:** the default flipped at `ce7fc637` (23:09) before the re-baseline, comparing **two** baselines that are byte-identical rather than 512 unchanged ones — §5 carries the corrected sequencing and D96 the reading; “opt-in until the map exists” is exactly what happened. With 18 handlers over 281 routes ~94% of the suite would skip, and a green suite over nothing is the failure D43 names one level up; flipping early would also make “baselines unchanged” unfalsifiable, because the suite would compare almost nothing | One branch condition in `fixtures.ts` and the rule in G1’s paragraph |
| D49 | Whether the missing 501 map is produced by a gate or by an instrument | **Answered 17:26 — an instrument: a dispatch-only Playwright job with `E2E_APP=v4`, whose output is data, never a gate.** The map’s keys are test title paths, so only a suite that runs can fill it honestly, and a job whose expected output is ~94% skips cannot be a gate. `skip-list:check` keeps policing the map on every push, so the instrument produces and the push-time check polices. The one-pixel control has a page — `login.png` exists in all 8 projects — but no runner on this box: the project pins `@playwright/test` 1.65.1, there is no root `node_modules`, and the only install is 1.60.0 from another repo, so its red has to be produced by the pinned version inside the instrument job (**D51**) | A workflow to be proposed by the builder; the map stays empty until it runs |
d rendered the built-ins; v4 lets the read fail, because an install that has migrated cannot be “no table yet”, and masking an outage as defaults offers a roles editor that cannot write.
