| D40 | Whether a line number quoted in a message is a fact or a claim | **Answered 14:22 — a claim, read it at the tree before it enters this plan.** Revision 51 carried the builder's G1 survey into the gate paragraph without opening the file; the two numbers the gate depends on were the two that were wrong (`:125` for the Docker branch, which opens at `:128`; `:34` for `CI4_UNPORTED`, declared at `:33`). Cheap to check, expensive to inherit | A reader who sizes G1 from the plan inherits the error, and the plan is where the build is measured against |
| D41 | Which copy governs when a revision body and a durable section disagree | **Answered 14:33 — the section; the stack stays append-only, and no operative anchor lives only in a revision body.** Revision 52 corrected the two anchors in its own text and left revision 51's false copies standing in the same file, in the paragraph a reader lands on first. Rewriting 51 would have made the record lie about what was believed at 14:22 and hidden the class of defect rather than one instance | One rule, one precedence sentence, and the anchors re-homed into Gate G1 |
| D42 | How a revision header's time relates to the commit that carries it | **Answered 16:02 — the header is never later than its commit.** Revision 53 was stamped 14:33 against a commit at 14:31:13, the discipline broken in the header that wrote it down. The clock is read before writing and the commit lands after; a header found ahead is corrected forward, never force-pushed into place, and the check is `git log -1 --format=%ci` | The plan's own timestamps are the evidence it quotes elsewhere |
| D43 | Where the `Ci4E2eSkipList` replacement runs, given v4's tests cannot read the tree | **Answered 16:16 — a `v4/scripts/check-*.mjs` in the unit job, not a Playwright test.** v4's vitest pool is workerd, so a v4 spec cannot touch `node:fs` (8 hits, all in `v4/scripts/`). The proposed home was Playwright enumeration in `snapshots.yml`, which renders baselines on dispatch or a label and never runs on a push — and `ci.yml` on this branch has no Playwright job | A guard that never runs on a push is not a guard; `v4.yml:68–83` already runs five tree-reading checks on push and PR. **Landed at `9920a38f`** as `check-visual-skip-list.mjs`: 17-case self-test, 43 specs, 155 titles, 0 keys, 3 unresolvable sites recorded by source text and checked in both directions, three controls (stale key, near-miss, removed record); the hand-off assertion from the retired test is still owed (revision 58) |
| D44 | What an anchor in this plan is, given code commits move line numbers | **Answered 16:33 — a (file, symbol, line, commit) quadruple, and a code commit that moves one re-homes it in the next docs revision.** G1's first increment added four lines to `tests/visual/fixtures.ts` and moved all eight seams the gate paragraph cited; the durable copy was the governing one (D41) and wrong in eight places within the hour it was written. The symbol survives the move (`spawn('php'` can be found again), the commit says which tree the line was counted in (`git show <commit>:<file>`), and the fix belongs to the next docs revision rather than the code commit, so no increment is gated on prose | A line number alone is a claim that decays silently; eight at once is not a corner case |
| D45 | What a sentence describing a check may claim, and where a count inside a ledger note is read | **Answered 16:47 — the check's actual scope, and the note carries its commit.** `docs/HANDOFF.md` said a key's page must be named "on this line"; `handoffVerdict()` tests `handoff.includes(title)` over the whole file, which is faithful to the retired `assertStringContainsString($handoff)`. The code is right and the prose overstates it, so the prose is what changes. The same commit's ledger note said "22 self-test cases" where the check prints 23 — D34 applied to a note: a count names its unit and the commit it was read at, or it is a claim | A sentence stricter than the check is a rule nobody enforces, and a count in a note is read as fact by whoever triages the row |
| D46 | What carries a project’s current state when the tracking issue’s body and a later comment disagree | **Answered 17:05 — the body, and it is rebuilt whenever the head moves; a comment is a log entry, never a substitute.** #367’s body sat at revision 55 / `ee427477` for 45 minutes while six commits landed and its newest comment reported revision 59 at `99998e1f`, so anyone opening the issue read stale state at exactly the place reading starts. The alternative is worse than the rewrite: a reader would have to know which comment is newest, and the body drifts silently because nothing checks it. Reach: one body rebuild per head movement, asserted by grepping the current revision and head out of the body after the API edit | One rule, and #367’s body rebuilt to revision 60 |
| D55 | What the re-baseline’s control must catch, now that the mixed era has a cause | **Answered 18:03 — a project-filtered render, and the control is built on the fifteen.** `1f86dd66` did not re-render “five PNGs” at random: it re-rendered **one project of four**. Measured at `4a187d0f`, `autofill-settings.spec.ts` carries the new `#fdfcfa` at 85–90% of its pixels in `desktop-chromium` and the old `#f5f5f7` at 47–57% in `chromium-pixel`, `webkit-iphone-se` and `webkit-iphone13` — 5 new, 15 stale, one spec — which is what `scripts/update-snapshots.sh`’s documented `-- --project=desktop-chromium` produces. So D53’s three controls get a fourth: **the fifteen siblings must change**. The three alone all pass a render that repeats the mistake; the fourth is the only one aimed at the failure that happened. The renderer is `main`’s `snapshots.yml` dispatched at `ref=main` (no commit touches `main`; the file is already at `4a187d0f`), fetched with `GH_TOKEN` set through `scripts/update-snapshots.sh --from-ci <run>` — `gh` is installed here but not logged in — and it had not been dispatched when this row was written — *corrected 18:52 on 2026-11-25: it **had**, successfully (`40108973286`, 2026-10-24, 773/773 in 18.6 min); what had never been exercised is the path on today’s app — see **D59***. The set is 512 = 4 light projects × 69 + 4 dark × 59, and the baselines are byte-identical between `main` `4a187d0f` and `547fe764` (tree `3fc937f9`) with no spec differing, so the PNG-only commit is a clean diff. |
| D56 | How a moved log entry and a paragraph naming the plan’s revision are kept true | **Answered 18:07 — a moved entry keeps its chronology, and the roadmap’s plan-of-record paragraph is compared in the same pass as the plan header.** Revision 65 moved revisions 60–62 into §9 in the order it found them in — 62, 61, 60 — so the log read 59, 62, 61, 60, 63…65; the entries were untouched and only their order changed. And the paragraph in `roadmap.md` that names the plan’s revision still said revision 49 at `9a3e2091` while the head carried 65 — sixteen revisions behind — after the same paragraph had been corrected at 14:10 for the same reason. Neither is a decision about the product; both are the record lying to a reader, which is why they cost a revision rather than a shrug. The hourly check already reads the plan header, so the roadmap line is one more comparison in a pass that runs anyway. |
| D57 | Whether a line number, a count or a clock time in a peer’s report can enter the plan as given | **Answered 18:26 — no: it is a claim, re-measured at the named commit first, and a commit message is a durable claim too.** The seeding report gave `fixtures.ts` as 240 lines with eight anchors (`:34`, `:70`, `:100`, `:174`, `:199`, `:219`/`:220`, `:231`, `:245`/`:247`); the file at `294e5a1f` is 258 lines with its seams at `:45`, `:85`, `:119`, `:170`/`:175`, `:182`, `:190`, `:210`/`:211`, `:230`/`:231`/`:234`, `:251`/`:253` — no uniform shift, so the list matched no version of the file, and the same message’s clock time named a moment that had not arrived. Anchors are re-homed by measurement, always (**D44**); the peer’s own follow-up, written after `grep -n`, reproduced exactly and added `sql()` `:119`, which is why the rule costs it one command rather than the plan a wrong number. |
| D58 | What to do with a wrong count inside a commit message, after `294e5a1f`’s said `fixtures.ts` went “225 to 240 lines” where it went 225 → 258 | **Answered 18:32 — corrected forward, never by rewriting the message.** A commit message is a durable claim (**D57**) and also history: amending it to fix a number rewrites what a reader of the branch saw. So the correction is a later sentence naming the true figures — a follow-up commit message or a plan note — and the wrong number stays where it was written. The same applies to a revision body: corrections are new revisions, and the old text stands. |
| D59 | Whether a render path that runs only on a dispatch or a label is covered by CI, after `snapshots.yml` silently stopped being able to render | **Answered 18:52 — no: it is exercised by a run, never by existing, and the CI4 cutover is the proof.** The workflow last rendered on 2026-10-24 (`40108973286`, 773/773 in 18.6 min) against the self-bootstrapping v3 app; `c49a865b` (2026-10-29) moved the front controller to CodeIgniter, which requires `vendor/`, and `15547ecb` deleted `src/bootstrap.php`, while `snapshots.yml` kept its old step list. Nothing failed for four weeks, because its only triggers are `workflow_dispatch` and a `pull_request` **label** — and every labelled run skips. | After a runtime cutover the render path is re-run before it is trusted; while `main` is frozen, running it means a branch off `main` with a `.github`-only diff |
| D60 | Whether 512 — the baseline count — is also the number of executions a render performs | **Answered 18:52 — no: 512 baselines, 810 executions.** 512 = 4 light projects × 69 + 4 dark × 59 files; the suite executes every light project on every spec, the dark projects’ `visual*`/theme match and `pgsql-smoke`, which is the `Running 810 tests using 3 workers` line in the log. | A DoD says “all 512 filenames” for coverage and “810 executions” for cost; a timeout is executions × per-test time, measured once at 773 in 18.6 min |
| D61 | What makes a step copied from another workflow portable | **Answered 18:52 — nothing: it carries the source job’s literals, so the version it installs is not the version it names.** `php8.3-intl` was copied from `ci.yml`’s e2e job, whose `Install PHP ${{ matrix.php }}` supplies 8.4; in a job that installs 8.3 it fetches the wrong extension. And `composer install` succeeding is not the app booting: `composer.json`’s `config.platform.php` 8.4.26 lets the install pass under 8.3 while the app answers 503. | The render installs `scripts/e2e-container-setup.sh 8.4` and `php8.4-intl`, and a copied step is diffed against its source job, literal by literal |
| D62 | Whether a green cheap pair says anything about the baselines it rendered | **Answered 19:04 — no: `--update-snapshots=all` writes, it never compares.** Playwright logged `is re-generated, writing actual` and `1 passed (2.9s)` for `visual-login.spec.ts` on `desktop-chromium`, having skipped the assertion entirely. So a cheap pair is the cheapest possible proof of the **boot path** — 8.4, `vendor/` present, `/login` rendering — and evidence for a baseline only through the four controls (byte-identical / must-change / twice-identical / planted). Anyone quoting “1 passed” as a re-baseline result is quoting a write. |
| D63 | Whether a stale baseline can be predicted from the project it belongs to, after five `desktop-chromium` files were found current and fifteen siblings stale | **Answered 19:04 — no: staleness is per file.** The mixed set has a mechanism (a `--project=` re-render, D55) but no project-level rule: `desktop-chromium/visual-login.spec.ts/login.png` is stale too, differing from a fresh render in 1,098,320 of 1,100,800 pixels while its `autofill-settings` siblings are current. Controls are therefore stated over named files, and control (a) names five files, not “the project”. |
| D64 | Whether “byte-identical” can be asserted across a whole render, after two runs of the same commit disagreed on 22 files | **Answered 19:21 — no: byte-identity is per file.** Two runs differing only in a `#login-form` rule disagree on 22 of 512 files by 1–59 pixels (RMSE ≤ 4.3e-4, `select.png` PAE 0.157), while the two cheap pairs’ `desktop-chromium/login.png` were byte-identical. So the control that certifies a re-baseline is Playwright’s own comparator on the flip gate; byte-identity is asserted only for files measured stable across two runs (control (a)’s five are). |
| D65 | What makes a render artifact a complete set, after a run with one failed test still produced 512 files | **Answered 19:27 — file provenance, not pass count.** An artifact is complete when every one of its files was written by that run or is byte-identical to the same file from a complete run of the same commit; a failed test disqualifies it only if it owns a baseline. `qa-v2.spec.ts` has no `toHaveScreenshot` call, and the five identical files match the all-passing plant artifact byte for byte. |
| D66 | Whether the flip gate’s “zero diff on the pages v4 implements” can be byte equality | **Answered 19:27 — no: it is a threshold.** Two runs of the same app, differing only in a `#login-form` rule, disagree on 22 of 512 files by 1–96 pixels (PAE ≤ 10,280 of 65,535), while the material change is 46,411–74,988 pixels per file. **Superseded in part by D67 (19:49):** a file counts as changed when **AE > 5,000 pixels** — 7.5× above the worst noise measured twice (663 px, two unplanted renders) and 9.3× below the smallest material change measured (46,411 px). The 22-file / 1–96 px measurement above stands as the plant-pair figure. |
| D67 | What the flip gate's change threshold is, after two unplanted renders of the same commit differed on 29 files by up to 663 px | **Answered 19:49 — AE > 5,000 pixels, and the justification is measured twice.** Two unplanted renders of `45660066518` vs `45663939402` differ on **29 of 512** files by **1–663 px** (worst `chromium-pixel-dark/visual-batch5.spec.ts/photo-editor.png`), while the smallest material change measured is **46,411 px**. 1,000 sat 1.5× above noise — one unlucky third render from failing a correct build — so the threshold moves to 5,000: **7.5× above noise, 9.3× below content.** Provisional: it is a workaround for nondeterminism, so making two renders agree is its own row, and when they do, the threshold tightens rather than disappears. |
| D68 | Which carrier renders the baselines, once `snapshots.yml` on frozen `main` is proven unable to | **Answered 19:49 — a branch off `main` with a `.github`-only diff (`rb-render-v3`, `6313e823`), and the v3 defect is filed, not fixed here.** The file needs the `composer install` `ci.yml` performs, PHP 8.4 (the app refuses 8.3 with a 503), and a timeout that is headroom rather than a fix. `scripts/update-snapshots.sh`'s local path inherits the same 8.3 default, so the documented path is broken too. It is v3's file on a frozen branch: recorded as v3 work with the three literals named, never landed under v4. It also carries an `extra_args` dispatch input interpolated into the shell command that runs Playwright — harmless on a branch whose whole job is one render, a hole the day the file is promoted — so the input is stripped before any copy of it becomes the durable carrier. |
| D69 | Whether a test that is load-sensitive under the render configuration and owns no baseline blocks G1 | **Answered 19:49 — no, but it is named, scoped and scheduled.** `qa-v2.spec.ts:56` (`selectOption` on `#stk-loc`, 45 s, `webkit-iphone13`) fails in both unsharded full renders and passed on `main`'s sharded e2e job at `62fe1c17`, so it is load-sensitive under the render configuration, not a branch-wide red. A baseline gate is not a suite gate (D65), so it does not block G1; the default flip must not inherit a red suite, so it is fixed before the flip on its own row. (Question reworded at 19:53 in revision 74: written before the measurement, it said “fails deterministically”, which this answer refutes — D70.) **Resolved 21:58 — the cause is named and the fix is in the spec.** The location add is a **POST + 303** (`modules/Taxonomy/Controllers/ManageController.php:61`, at `4a187d0f`) and `/stocktake` renders `#stk-loc` **server-side** from the locations table (`modules/Stocktake/Views/StocktakeView.php:90`), so a missing option means the location was never created: the spec clicked add and navigated away on the **next line**, and under load the POST was aborted before it landed. It now waits for the redirect’s own confirmation — `.toast` containing “Location added”, which is `ManageView.php:109` rendering `Kind.php:19`’s `'One' => 'Location'` — verified here at `4a187d0f` rather than taken from the patch. My “load-sensitive, not broken” was therefore half right: intermittent under load, and the intermittency was a **spec** race the load exposed, not a tolerance to live with. Fixed at `a6018166`. |
| D70 | Whether a decision row's question may stand as written when its own answer refutes it | **Answered 19:53 — no: both halves are corrected in one edit, and the row says when.** D69 asked about a test that “fails deterministically” and answered “load-sensitive”; a question is a claim too, and two claims in one row read as two findings to whoever consults it next. The question is reworded to the measured fact, the finding is untouched, and the edit is dated in the row. This is not a breach of D58: a revision body is history and is never rewritten, but a durable §-section's job is to carry current truth (D41), so it is corrected where it is read and the correction is dated where it was made. |
| D71 | Whether a `continue-on-error` map job's conclusion is evidence | **Answered 20:02 — no; its artifact is** (stamp corrected from 20:06 in revision 77, D73). The job exists to answer what v4 says and is expected to be mostly red, so "red" and "v4 never started" share a conclusion; the report's shape separates them (0 tests, or every test dead at `waitUp`/`/readyz`, is a boot failure), and a boot failure reported as a map is the D61 defect one file later. |
| D72 | Whether the `v4-unported` annotation is a proven carrier | **Answered 20:02 — unproven until a report carries one** (stamp corrected from 20:06 in revision 77, D73). `fixtures.ts` pushes it on `info.annotations` after `await use()`, so every map key depends on the array reaching the JSON report; if a run's report has no such annotation while pages answer 501 inside it, the mechanism is vacuous and the carrier, not the classifier, is what gets fixed. |
| D73 | Whether a revision header's time may be typed by hand | **Answered 20:06 — no: the script that writes the header reads `date`, and the stamp is checked against `git log -1 --format=%ci` before the push.** Revision 76 stamped 20:06 against a commit at 20:02:56, which makes D42 a sentence rather than a mechanism. A stamp not yet published may be amended in place (revision 70 did); a published one is corrected forward, and the durable rows then carry the corrected time with the revision that fixed it named. |
| D74 | Whether `ci4-unported.json` can be filled by the run that reads it | **Answered 20:43 — no: the skip list is a sink, not a source, and the circular instruction was mine.** `tests/visual/fixtures.ts:251` (`842adc68`) skips a test when its key **is** in the file, so with `{}` nothing skips and nothing self-reports; *“skipped keys become entries”* cannot bootstrap, and no redesign of the skip is needed — a skip list records what is already known unported. What was missing is a **source**: the `v4-unported` annotation per 501 plus the named `fixture()`/`sql()`/`image()`/`settings` refusals, measured by a run and never by a hand (D49). The map job harvests that evidence into `ci4-unported.proposed.json`; promotion into the real file is a **second commit**, gated by `skip-list:check`, with the key count in its message. Measured at `690c956f`: 170 tests, 106 keys (95 / 10 / 1), 2 served, 50 skipped, 12 unexplained. |
| D75 | Whether a page v4 implements but renders differently belongs in the map | **Answered 20:43 — no: it is a third bucket, enumerated and decided.** Run `45671253354` shows `visual.spec.ts` “error page”: v4 answers `/nope` itself and the page differs from the committed `not-found.png` by **14,730 px (2%)**, so it is neither unported (the route exists and answers) nor render noise (D67's worst measured noise is 663 px). The bucket is **served-but-different**: every member is named with its AE and either fixed in v4 or re-baselined with a written reason — never absorbed by the map, because a skip would flip a page that renders wrong and call the flip done. |
| D76 | Whether a 501 a spec reads through `page.request` belongs to the map | **Answered 21:03 — yes, and the carrier must see it.** `qa-fixes.spec.ts:6` reads `/new` with `page.request.get(…)`, gets v4's 501, finds no `new_location` field and fails with `expected 1, received 0` — and the run's report carries **no annotation for it**, because `page.on('response')` and `browser.newContext()`'s `ctx.on('response')` do not fire for `APIRequestContext` calls. The map's source is *every 501 v4 answered* (D74), so the fixture records from the request context too; until it does, that red stays `unexplained` and is named as a carrier gap rather than a mystery. Wrapping `newContext` alone cannot close it: the test fails before it opens a context. |
| D77 | Whether a test that dies because v4 failed to boot is a map failure | **Answered 21:03 — no: boot is its own bucket, and it blocks the flip, not the map.** One of 170 tests (`attachments.spec.ts:6`) failed with `wrangler dev exited (1): ERROR Address already in use (socket "direct:1:default")` — v4 exited during boot, so nothing about the page was measured, and a flip gate whose reds can be a port collision cannot attribute a red to a page. Boot is therefore proven per test (a recorded `/readyz` hit) rather than assumed, a boot failure is reported with the test named, and the port allocation is the first suspect — `waitPortClosed` exists today for the restart path, not for boots that race. |
| D78 | Whether a map measured by a run that lost a boot can be used alone | **Answered 21:23 — no: the map is a union across boot-clean runs.** Two runs over the same tree produced **106** and **114** keys with **+9 / −1** against an intersection of 105: the nine are the four `php`-spawning tests and the five context-openers that now attribute their 501, and the minus is `auth.spec.ts` “login, wrong password, logout @smoke” — a 501 key in run `45671253354` that run `45677452961` lost to `wrangler dev exited (1) … Address already in use (socket "direct:1:default")`. A dead boot therefore **subtracts** a measured key, so a run containing any `wrangler dev exited` is a partial set that looks complete: the map is the union of keys measured by boot-clean runs (**106 ∪ 114 = 115**), each key carries the run that measured it, and the crash cannot be excluded by test name because it moved from `attachments.spec.ts` to `auth.spec.ts` between the two. |
| D79 | Whether promoting the map may land as a map-only commit | **Answered 21:23 — no: the same commit carries `docs/HANDOFF.md`’s bullet.** `skip-list:check` fails a key whose test title `docs/HANDOFF.md` does not contain, and fails the bullet while it still says the list “is empty”, so map and hand-off couple by construction. The promotion is therefore **one commit** holding `tests/visual/ci4-unported.json` (**115 keys**, the count in the message) and the rewritten hand-off bullet, green under `skip-list:check` — an in-repo doc must be true at the commit it ships in, the same rule the workflow comment was corrected under. |
| D80 | Whether a guard may record a test title no static parser can read | **Answered 21:32 — yes, but only two-directionally.** `manage.spec.ts` builds its titles in a loop (`for (const kind of [“locations”,“categories”])`), so the parser cannot read them and two legitimate keys (`manage.spec.ts: manage categories › add, rename, default, delete-with-move` and the `locations` twin that alone carries `@smoke`) looked identical to keys for tests that do not exist. `DYNAMIC_KEYS` records the site and its exact titles and is checked in both directions — read in `verdict()` at `e47543c4`, not taken from a report: a key outside the record is still an error (with a closest-match hint), a recorded title no key uses is “the record is stale”, and a recorded site absent from the corpus is an error. Sanctioned: it widens exactly those two keys and closes behind them. The cost it bought is `docs/HANDOFF.md` 59 → 227 lines, which is a carrier problem, not a guard problem. |
| D81 | Whether a v4 harvest report can prove that each test got a live app (D71) | **Answered 21:32 — no: it yields a floor, and 67 of 170 are indeterminate by construction.** Read at `e47543c4` from the two harvest reports: **101** tests provably had a live v4 (99 carrying a 501 annotation plus the 2 that passed), **2** provably did not (`attachments.spec.ts` “Markdown notes render safely” and `auth.spec.ts` “login, wrong password, logout”, both `wrangler dev exited (1): … Address already in use (socket “direct:1:default”)`), and **67** cannot be decided from the report at all because they need no page to fail — 50 skipped themselves, 10 die in the `sql` fixture, 1 in `settings`, 4 shell `php`. The fix is not a classifier change: one `v4-booted` annotation per test, pushed by the same carrier that records the 501, turns the floor into a census. A row, not a flip blocker — the flip gate compares baselines and does not consume a boot count. |
| D82 | Whether a spec that shells `php` belongs in the map as a key | **Answered 21:58 — no: it skips in the spec, because the map is a map of pages.** Four specs shell `php` themselves — `autofill-on-demand`, `autofill-settings`, `autofill-suggest` (three `spawn php`) and `originals.spec.ts` (the fixture's own `spawnSync php`) — and a key cannot silence them: a key is evaluated inside the test's fixtures, so `info.skip()` cannot cancel a failure raised by another fixture or by the test's own shell-out. Measured once rather than assumed: declaring `ci4Unported` first in the fixture object, so the skip runs before `reset` boots v4, moved the run from 110 to 111 skips and left the php specs red — the ordering was not the cause and was not kept. The four therefore `test.skip(APP === 'v4', …)` in their own files and the map goes **115 → 111**; run `45689306268` shows the arithmetic at work — **161 skips = 107 under the map + 54 by the specs' own conditions** (50 + the four). They are named in `docs/HANDOFF.md`'s skip section and in §5 G1, so nothing is lost by dropping the keys: the map records pages, not binaries. |
| D83 | Whether the `CI4_UNPORTED` lookup may be ungated | **Answered 21:58 — no, and the check now pins the gate.** `15547ecb` dropped the `APP === 'ci4'` guard when the app name became `v4` (`264e3dc3` had it), and with an empty map nothing noticed; with 115 keys, every v3 run — the flip gate and the `update-snapshots` renderer — would have skipped every listed test. The builder's figure for what that costs was **8 baseline-owning tests**; measured here from the spec sources at `e47543c4` it is **22** mapped tests whose body renders at least one baseline (20 in `visual`/`visual-settings`/`visual-features`/`visual-batch5`, counting a shared `shot()` helper rather than a literal `toHaveScreenshot` call, plus 2 autofill). The gate is restored and `v4/scripts/check-visual-skip-list.mjs` fails when it is absent; verified here by removing it (**exit 1, one finding, named**) and restoring it (**green**: 111 keys, 43 specs, 155 titles, 3 unresolvable sites, 26 self-test cases). |
| D84 | What one dead v4 boot costs a run | **Answered 21:58 — a worker's remaining tail, not one test.** Run `45689306268` at `a1f4fa82`, read from its artifact: 170 tests, expected 2, skipped 161, unexpected 7, flaky 0; **four of the seven reds are one collision** — `wrangler dev exited (1) … Address already in use (socket "direct:1:default")` on `add.spec.ts` “photo from the camera input…” and on three consecutive `visual-features.spec.ts` tests, so a single port race cost four tests in one worker. The remaining three are `attachments.spec.ts` “Markdown notes render safely @smoke” at a 501 for `/item/1/edit` that no run had mapped, `qa-fixes` (D76) and `visual › error page` (D75). Two consequences: boot collisions block the flip **ahead of** D75 and D76, because a gate whose reds are random victims cannot attribute a red to a page — the fix is a port per boot, not a retry; and **a dead boot hides keys as well as subtracting them** (D78's other direction), since `/item/1/edit` was invisible for four runs only because every earlier run lost that test to a boot death before it reached its page. A promotion therefore needs a **boot-clean** run, and the harvest is now cheap enough to repeat — 1 m 57 s of tests, because 161 skip instantly. |
| D85 | What the `Address already in use` collision actually is | **Answered 22:05 — the inspector socket, not the app port.** `startV4` already takes its port from `freePort()` (`tests/visual/v4-server.cjs`), so the fixed one was the other socket; the seam now passes `--inspector-port 0`. Verified rather than accepted: `inspector-port` is a real `wrangler dev` option (`v4/node_modules/wrangler/wrangler-dist/cli.js:193741`) and `validatePort` accepts **0** (`:379418–379425`), so the argv is legal. The survival counts (four concurrent boots: 1/3 and 2/4 without the flag, 4/4 with, 4/4 staggered by 6 s) are the builder's measurement and date from its run, not from this row. Consequence: the promotion re-bases on a boot-clean harvest, because `/item/1/edit` is a measured 501 that none of the 111 keys at `a1f4fa82` names — 111 is a floor, not the final size. |
| D86 | Whether a listed key that can never fire is a key | **Answered 22:14 — no: the map’s size is its *effective* keys.** Run `45691829738` at `f28b0d32`, read from artifact `15059137398`: 165 skips decompose as **100** “v4 answers 501 for …” + **10** “the `sql` fixture has no v4 equivalent” + **55** “the spec shells `php` itself”, i.e. **110 applied, not 111**. The missing one is `originals.spec.ts › a photo over the upload limit says the original is not kept`, reason “the `settings` fixture has no v4 equivalent”: since that spec now skips wholesale for `php` (D82), its map fixture never runs and the key is listed and dead. Nothing is lost by dropping it — the spec’s own skip and `docs/HANDOFF.md`’s php note carry the same fact — while keeping it makes every count of “keys applied” disagree with the file. So the promotion is **111 keys, all effective**, and any key whose reason cannot appear in a harvest is a defect, not a record. |
| D87 | Whether a boot-clean harvest proves the boot fix | **Answered 22:14 — no: necessary, not sufficient.** That run has only **five tests that boot v4** (2 expected + 3 unexpected); the other 165 skip before the fixture, and the collision that took four tests in `45689306268` needs concurrent boots. The proof is the seam’s own measurement (four concurrent boots: 1/3 and 2/4 without the flag, 4/4 with, 4/4 staggered by 6 s) plus the pin in `check-visual-boot.mjs` (removing the flag: exit 1, `FAIL the seam lets the inspector port be chosen`; restoring: 8 cases green). The stress test is a harvest whose map is much smaller — which is what the port produces as pages are served — so the port allocation is re-checked then, not now. *(Corrected 22:45, D88: “five tests boot v4” was inferred from the tests that **ran**, and the census measures **115 of 170** booting — a mapped test boots before its fixture skips. So the harvest is the stress test, and it came back with zero deaths.)* |
| D88 | Whether a harvest boots v4 often enough to stress the seam | **Answered 22:45 — yes: 115 of 170 tests, and it is measured rather than inferred.** D87 counted the tests that *ran* in a harvest (2 expected + 3 unexpected = 5) and concluded the boot path was barely exercised; the census shows a mapped test skips **inside its fixture, after the boot**, so the count is 170 − 55 php self-skips = **115** `v4-booted` annotations. Run `45697085355` at `0acfbc26` records those 115 and **zero** boot deaths, where `45689306268` at the pre-fix harness lost four tests to one collision. The durable rule: **a boot count is read from the report’s annotations, never from the run’s pass/fail arithmetic** — and a harvest under the map is itself a seam stress test, so D87’s “upcoming stress test” was already being spent. |
| D89 | Where a CI-only gate is named, and whether a wider window is the fix | **Answered 22:48 — beside the six, in §6; and no, the window is a clock.** `docs/HANDOFF.md` carries no gate list at all (`git grep -i gate` at `0acbc26`, nothing), so revision 85's order to name `check-visual-boot.mjs` there was misaddressed by me: the pre-push list is §6's D30 paragraph, and it now names `visual-boot:check` as CI-only — run by `v4.yml:127`, the `workerd smoke` job's step 6 — because it boots workerd and the six do not. `82f9d2db` widened the self-test's slice from 400 to 1600 characters, and that is why `45694333366`'s red is gone, but the defect was the slice: any fixed window is a clock the next added line stops. **Corrected 23:28 (D97):** the scan to `reset: [`'s closing bracket **landed at `23009fd9`** — `resetBlock()` at `v4/scripts/check-visual-boot.mjs:56`, 10 self-test cases (brackets inside comments and inside strings, a fragment with no closing bracket refused), the block 715 characters with the php seed at 620 — so nothing is owed here. The row read “stays an open order” for twenty minutes after the commit existed, which is D95's class, not a second order. `23009fd9` also put the gate into `docs/HANDOFF.md`; `1e1e218d` removed it, because §6 is the single carrier. |
| D90 | Whether a one-pixel control can prove a 5,000-pixel comparator | **Answered 23:04 — no, and the rule that made it impossible is the rule it served.** §5's G1 paragraph and my DoD both asked for “a deliberate one-pixel change” to go red while §6's comparator rule is `maxDiffPixels: 5_000`; 1 < 5,000, so the plant is green by construction and a control that demanded red would have forced either the budget down or the control to lie. The builder named the contradiction, planted **6,000** (budget + 1,000), asserted the reported count exceeds the budget and that the message names the baseline, then restored the file byte for byte — and both compared baselines went red at exactly 6,000 with a **base difference of 0** (`45700026586`, job log `139507404012`). The durable sentence is now “larger than the budget”; the locating property (one pixel is found and counted) survives as a library self-test, not as a render | The control's size is a property of the budget, so it moves with it; a one-pixel plant can never be the flip's evidence |
| D91 | Whether a workflow file Actions cannot parse is caught locally | **Answered 23:04 — no, and it costs a run to find out.** `45699752450` at `d7fe8363`: created 20:56:51Z, failed 20:56:51Z, **zero jobs**, run name equal to the workflow file's path. Cause: a step name containing `: ` inside a plain YAML scalar starts a mapping, so the file did not parse. `workflows:check` scans workflow text for PHP steps and does not load YAML, so the six local gates could not see it — a seventh instance of D89's shape, one level up: not a gate that runs elsewhere, but a file no gate reads as a file. Fixed at `98c94dbb` (a dash where the colon was) and proved by the next run parsing; the durable order is that every workflow YAML is loaded by `workflows:check`, with a `: `-in-a-scalar fixture case | One line of YAML can cost a whole run's evidence, and “six gates green” says nothing about whether Actions can load the file |
| D92 | Whether the flip's gate certifies the branch the flip lands on | **Answered 23:04 — not while the trigger excludes it, so the landing commit carries the trigger.** `visual-v4.yml` fires on pushes to `v4/visual-instrument` and `v4/g1-map`, and its own comment says the flip is “a gate for the pushes this file can see, not for experimental-v4”. The flip changes the default harness for the branch of record, so a verdict that cannot fire there certifies the branch where the change was *developed*, not the branch where it *lives*. The landing commit adds `experimental-v4` to the branches list and gates `instrument` and `map` off it per job, so one job runs per push to the branch of record and the 40-minute harvest stays on its own branch | Cost is one job per push to the branch of record; the alternative is a flip whose regression mode nobody measures |
| D93 | Whether a durable sentence may point at another section for a rule | **Answered 23:07 — only if that section carries it, and a rule belonging to an unlanded increment says so in the same sentence with the branch's current value beside it.** Revision 87 wrote “under §6's 5,000-pixel rule” while §6 had no comparator paragraph and `playwright.config.ts` at `f6f61780` still ran `maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.002` — the same class as D89, one hour after I filed it against the builder: a pointer in prose is a claim, and the file it points at is the evidence. §6 now carries the rule with both values (`98c94dbb` where it is measured, `f6f61780` where this branch stands) | A reader sizing the flip from the plan would have found a rule §6 never stated and a config that never ran it |
| D94 | Whether a landing's gate is the run the landing produced, and how a trigger's guards are evidenced | **Answered 23:15 — yes, and by skipped jobs, never by reading the YAML.** `68d3401c` landed the flip, its comparator and the `experimental-v4` trigger in one push; run `45701796669` shows the flip job green 21:10:05→21:13:26Z with `170 tests / 3 executed / 167 skipped (112 with a v4 boot)` and both baselines red at 6,000 with base 0, and shows `instrument` and `map` **skipped**. A guard that silently lets a job through is indistinguishable from one that works until the runner bill, so the evidence is the job list, not the `if:` text — and a gate that certifies where a change was developed is not a gate for the branch where it lives (D92) | A landing's evidence is its own run at its own tree |
| D95 | Whether a durable paragraph may predict | **Answered 23:19 — no: a prediction is state when written and false by default at the next landing.** Three of the six sentences corrected in revision 90 were forecasts of exactly that shape — a count another file owns (`workflows:check`'s three), an order between two landings (“the promotion … then the flip”), a row’s ledger status — and each was true when written and false when read. The rule is mechanical: every §-section sentence that predicts is re-read in the revision that records the landing, and a correction is dated inside the sentence (D70) rather than left for a report to find | A durable sentence that says “still owed”, “next” or “until” carries a commit’s authority without a commit’s freshness |
| D96 | What a green gate over a suite that mostly skips certifies | **Answered 23:19 — what it executed, and that number belongs in the commit message.** The flip ran **3 of 170** tests and compared **2 of 512** baselines, both byte-identical (base 0), so it certifies the harness path and the comparator rule over those two pages and says nothing about the other 510; every flip-related commit carries “two baselines, not 512” | A green run over 98% skips is the claim D43 forbids, and the flip is where it would be easiest to make |
| D97 | What “landed” means when a peer says a fix was already in | **Answered 23:28 — a property of origin, and only the push decides whether a report could have seen it.** `23009fd9` is authored 22:51:30, committed 23:09:54, and reached origin in the 23:10 push; revision 86 was committed 22:48:50. So “already landed when your revision said it was owed” cites a tree the branch of record could not read — and the durable consequence is unchanged, because the sentence said “still owed” and the next landing made it false (D95). Report the clock you measured against: author date, commit date and push are three different times (D57) | An author date is when someone’s tree changed, not when anyone could have read it |
| D98 | Whether a gate that loads YAML is proved by its self-test | **Answered 23:37 — no: by a planted file and an empty directory, run against the real tree.** At `0ffd7490` the self-test prints **12 cases, 0 failed**, but a self-test asserts its own fixtures; the proof that the gate guards the repo is three runs: the real directory (**11 workflow file(s) load as YAML; 4 can gate `experimental-v4`** — ci, snapshots, v4, visual-v4 — **no PHP step in any**, exit 0), a copy carrying `- name: Harvest: the map` (**exit 1**, file and line named, *“does not load as YAML, so Actions runs no job from this file (D91)”*), and an empty directory (**exit 1**, *“so this check proves nothing”*). The empty case matters most: a check that read no file has no standing to pass | A self-test can be green while the check it guards reads nothing |
| D99 | Whether the branch that carries a landing may trail the branch of record | **Answered 23:37 — yes, bounded by what the harvest executes, and the bound is named by path.** `v4/g1-map` (`68d3401c`) and `experimental-v4` (`0ffd7490`) differ by seven files: two docs, a **comment** in `playwright.config.ts`, `v4/docs/testing.md`, the `yaml` dev dependency, and the workflow checker. None is a spec, a fixture, the map or `v4/src`, so a harvest taken on the older tree measures the same app — and the cost of keeping them equal is a 28–40 minute run per commit, paid for nothing the harvest reads. The bound: the first commit touching `tests/visual/**`, `fixtures.ts`, `ci4-unported.json`, the comparator’s behaviour or `v4/src` recreates the branch at the head before the next map | Two trees are two versions of every file they share; the rule is which files may differ, not whether they may |
| D100 | Whether a job log that “will not fetch” is unavailable | **Answered 23:37, corrected 23:54 — no: the endpoint 302s to a signed storage URL, and two different failures follow from that one hop.** The builder twice reported a landed run’s log as empty or unfetchable and borrowed another run’s lines; one failure is a **401 on the redirect**, because a client that forwards the `Authorization` header hands it to storage, which authenticates by signature; the other is a **404 `BlobNotFound`**, because a job with no steps publishes no log, and a tool that leaves its previous file in place reads that failure as a short or stale body. Read the `Location` and download it without the header: job `139525758567` in `45705344087` is **27,867 characters / 28,039 bytes** and carries the shape line, the two compared baselines and all four control lines at **6,000 pixels / base 0**. So a report that cannot fetch its own evidence is a tooling defect to fix, never a reason to cite a different run — and the landed run’s evidence was one redirect away | D71’s artefact is only as good as the tool that reads it, and a borrowed quotation is a different run’s test |
| D101 | Whether a replaced number keeps the comparison it carried | **Answered 23:47 — yes, and this revision exists because mine did not.** Revision 92 replaced “0.2% of a phone viewport is 500 px” with the measured budgets **363 / 517 / 691 / 2,201** and then said they were below the 663 px noise floor “on all three phone projects”: true for the iPhone SE and iPhone 13, false for the Pixel 7, whose 691 px is above it. The right comparison — two of three — had been written to the builder an hour earlier. A number in a durable sentence is there to support a comparison; replacing it without re-stating that comparison replaces the claim, not the typo | A correction can be right about the digits and wrong about what they prove |
| D102 | In what unit a log’s size is quoted | **Answered 23:54 — bytes, measured on the raw bytes, with a character count named as one if it is quoted at all.** The flip job’s log is **27,867 characters and 28,039 bytes**: revision 92 quoted the first as the second, one revision after D101 made exactly that class a rule. The 172-byte difference is the UTF-8 headroom of **171 non-ASCII characters** — the byte-order mark plus the degree sign and middle dot — and not line endings, since the file is 341 lines with zero CRLF. A decoded length and a file’s stored size are different numbers, and the one that will be compared with another file’s size is the second | A length without its unit is a claim in the wrong unit |
| D103 | Whether a quoted size is an anchor at all | **Answered: only if the next reader reproduces it to the byte, so the boundary is named — BOM counted, terminating newline counted.** Measured here at 00:03 on job `139535305031`: **28,040 bytes / 27,868 characters** with the BOM and the final newline in, **27,867 characters** with the BOM stripped, **170** non-ASCII characters in that body, **341 lines, zero CRLF**. Revisions 92 and 94 both quoted this one log and both were short by the same byte, because they measured the stream minus its terminator; the 172-byte gap they explained was still correct, which is why the wrong number survived a review that checked the arithmetic. **(Corrected 00:12, revision 96: the two numbers were never the same object — revision 94 quoted job `139525758567`, **28,039 bytes**, and this row’s 28,040 belongs to job `139535305031`, a different flip job one byte longer in one line; revision 92’s “27,867 bytes” was the unit error D102 already named, not a missing terminator. See D104.)** | A size whose boundary is unstated is not reproducible, and a number nobody can reproduce is not an anchor |
| D104 | What identifies a quoted size | **Answered 00:12 — its object, with its boundary and a hash: a run and job id, the byte count with the BOM and the terminator counted, and the sha256 of the bytes.** Two flip jobs of consecutive runs are **28,040 bytes** (job `139535305031`, run **`45708142039`** — `visual-v4.yml` at `412b9859`; **this row first said `45708142150`, which is that commit’s `v4.yml` run and owns no flip job — corrected in place 00:21**, sha256 `747f21d8…`) and **28,039 bytes** (job `139525758567`, run `45705344087`, sha256 `f96821b5…`); both are 341 lines, zero CRLF, 171 non-ASCII. Revision 95 compared one against the other and concluded revision 94 had measured the same stream minus its terminator — false, and not repeatable by anyone holding either file. | A size quoted without its object invites exactly that comparison, and a one-byte gap then reads as a boundary error when it is two different jobs |
| D105 | Whether a green gate proves nothing crashed | **Answered: no — a workerd can die and leave every test green.** On 2026-11-25 at 23:56 a local gate run left `v4/core.37999` in the harness directory, **70 MB**, with no failed test anywhere; neither the six local gates nor the boot census can see that, because the census reads annotations and a crash that no test reported writes none. Two obligations follow: **the directory must be unable to accept the file** — `core` and `core.*` ignored in `v4/.gitignore`, which at `2b196b8b` names eight paths and no core pattern, `git check-ignore v4/core.37999` exiting 1 — and **a core file newer than the run’s start is reported as a finding** with its size and time, never deleted quietly. **Corrected forward 2026-11-26 00:41:** both obligations **landed at `7b4ab82e`** — `core` and `core.*` in `v4/.gitignore` (`git check-ignore v4/core.37999` now exits 0), and `v4/scripts/check-core-dumps.mjs` with `--since`/`--dir`, 12 self-test cases, exit 1 on a core at or after the run’s start and 0 on an older one, printed as `older` and never deleted. The reporting half is wired to `v4/` and the repo root only, so **coverage is the invocation, not the script** (D106), and the driver that runs it lives outside the repo (D107). | A 70 MB binary sat one `git add -A` from the history, and a crashed workerd that fails no test is exactly what a green colour hides |
| D106 | Whether a scan’s coverage follows from its script | **Answered: no — the coverage is the argv, and the script’s capability is silent about it.** `check-core-dumps.mjs` finds a core anywhere it is pointed, and it was pointed at `v4/` and the repo root — `2 director(ies) (/home/rakazo/apotheke/v4, /home/rakazo/apotheke)` — while the **393,977,856-byte** chromium core from 2026-11-25T20:00:50Z sits in `/home/rakazo`. The same binary with `--dir /home/rakazo` exits 1 and names it. So a green REPORT line is a claim about two directories, and it must say which ones it read; the gate scans the home as well, and the REPORT line names every directory it covered. **Still open at 01:01 (revision 100), re-measured rather than inherited:** the driver’s report line still reads `--dir . --dir ..` from `v4/`, the home-level scan has not landed, and `git ls-tree -r origin/experimental-v4 v4/scripts` has no `gates.sh` in it — so coverage is still the argv, and the argv is still two directories. **Corrected forward 2026-11-26 01:30: landed at `8e3f5fdc` and re-run here at `40b30aed`** — the driver passes `--dir "$HOME"` as a third directory, the FAIL and green lines both name every directory read, and the home-level scan reports the **393,977,856-byte** chromium core as `older` over **3 director(ies)**. | “the local scan now covers it” was said of a file it could not reach, and the next crash one level up would have been just as invisible |
| D107 | Where the local gate driver lives | **On one box, outside the repo, and that is a defect.** `/home/rakazo/gates.sh` is **1,140 B**, mtime 00:27, untracked — six `gate()` calls (tsc, biome, depcruise, routes, fixtures, vitest) plus one `report()` call (`--since`, `--dir . --dir ..`) — so “six gates plus a REPORT line” and the meaning of `--since` are reproducible only on this box. It is committed as `v4/scripts/gates.sh` with the run-start rule in a comment: the durable artefacts are nothing without the driver that runs them. **Still open at 01:01 (revision 100):** the file is unchanged — **1,140 B**, mtime 00:27, outside the repo — and the tree at `f81bdf12` carries 26 files under `v4/scripts` and not this one. | The last increment lost a worktree because it lived on one box; a harness whose seventh line exists in exactly one home is the same bet. **Corrected forward 2026-11-26 01:30: landed at `8e3f5fdc`** — `v4/scripts/gates.sh`, 60 lines, **mode 100755** at `40b30aed` (`git ls-tree` reads 100755; a first attempt committed it 644 and was amended before the push), resolving its tree from `${BASH_SOURCE[0]}` and its node from `$APOTHEKE_NODE_BIN`, a `node22/bin` beside the repo, then PATH |
| D108 | Whether a usage example is an anchor | **Answered: no — an example is a claim that survives being copied, so its argument must be one the reader’s clock produces.** `check-core-dumps.mjs:10` offers `--since 1764106138` as “only cores at or after that instant fail”; that epoch is **2025-11-25T21:28:58Z**, and the gate run it documents started **2026-11-25T22:27:40Z = 1795559260**. A reader who copies it selects every core the box ever kept and learns to ignore the line. The identical constant at `:135` tests parsing and stays. | A filter that fires on a year of history is a scare, and the next reader turns the check off |
| D109 | Whether a v3 test whose mechanism v4 replaced *by design* is ever ported | **Answered: no — it is `dropped` with the replaced code cited, and any half of it that v4 does assert is re-homed as a v4-native assertion.** S1.7’s two largest rows assert mechanisms that exist nowhere in `v4/src`: `DatabaseSessionHandlerTest` (26 methods) spends 16 on the stale-lock takeover, the claim protocol and the `lock_token`/`locked_until` columns, and `v4/src/session/store.ts:5-10` says the lock is *deliberately not ported* because a v4 request loads the row once and writes one batch; `UnitOfWorkTest` (26) asserts a callback-scoped unit — guards, holds on unwritten refs, savepoints, units joining and failing an outer transaction — and `grep -E "UnitOfWork\|savepoint"` over `v4/src` returns **0 files**; `UnitOfWorkLockTest` (9) is the same write lock. Flipping those rows to `ported` would claim **61 methods of evidence that does not exist**, so they are `dropped` with the reason naming the code, and the one half that is real — the 10 methods asserting row read/write/expiry/epoch — is re-homed into `v4/test/session/store.spec.ts`, with any of the 10 that has no counterpart added there as a v4-native assertion. The four **per-site batching** rows (`UnitOfWorkSitesTest` 8, `UnitOfWorkRemainingSitesTest` 5, `UnitOfWorkHistorySitesTest` 8, `UnitOfWorkRemainingSites2Test` 6, **27 methods** together) stay `scheduled`: v4 does batch, and which writes share a batch is exactly the edge case a fresh port misses. `UnitOfWorkRemainingSitesRaceTest` (21, 20 data rows) also stays, because its subject is atomicity across sites, which batches supply | A dropped row is a claim that v4 makes the assertion meaningless; a `ported` row is a claim that v4 makes it. The ledger may lose evidence, never invent it |
| D110 | Whether an instant may be written twice in one line | **Answered: no — once, in the form the reader can check.** `--since 1795559260  # 2026-11-25T22:27:40Z` is two claims about one instant, and they differ by **86,400 s** (1795559260 is 2026-11-24T22:27:40Z; 2026-11-25T22:27:40Z is 1795645660). Nothing in the line says which one the parser will use, and a base-10 epoch is not a number a human re-derives, so the pair read as one fact for two revisions. The parser already accepts ISO, so the example is written `--since 2026-11-25T21:28:58Z`: one instant, its own units, no conversion to disagree with. The same rule applies to any `--since`, any log timestamp and any `created_at` in a doc. **Landed at `40b30aed`, read here:** the example is `--since 2026-11-25T21:28:58Z    # the gate run above, 23:28:58 EET` — one instant, ISO, its own clock agreeing — and a **14th** self-test case reads the example back out of the file and fails on a disagreeing clock or a bare epoch. No artefact records that run's start, so the instant is the replaced constant's clock time and the comment says "the gate run above" rather than claiming a measurement | A number written twice is a number that can be wrong once |
| D111 | Whether a gate driver’s exit code is part of the gate | **Answered: yes, and this driver’s is not.** Run here on a copy of the committed `v4/scripts/gates.sh` with its six `gate` calls replaced by a command that fails and its one `report` call replaced by one that succeeds: it printed **six `GATE fail exit=1`** lines and **exited 0**. Bash’s status is the last command’s, the script does not accumulate, and `set -uo pipefail` does not abort, so the six lines say what a reader must read and the status says everything is fine. A driver that gates must exit non-zero if **any** gate did, keep the per-gate line, and never let the REPORT line decide the run’s status. Until then “six gates green” is a claim about reading six lines, which is what D71 asked of runs and never asked of the driver. **Landed at `40b30aed`, A/B run here on copies of the committed file:** six failing gates + a succeeding report → **exit 1** (`gates: 6 of 6 failed`); six passing gates + a failing report → **exit 0** (`gates: all 6 passed`); both red → **exit 1**. The builder also measured the old file's other direction — green gates with a red report exiting 1 — which its structure predicts (its `report` had no `return 0`); that one case I did not re-run on the old file | A green exit code is the most-read claim a driver makes |
| D112 | Whether a row count may be corrected in one field | **Answered: no — a row that leaves one status enters another, so both counters move and the three must sum to the total.** Revision 101 said D109's three rows take the ledger to **50 ported / 128 scheduled / 67 dropped**. A row cannot leave `scheduled` without landing in `dropped`, so 130−3=**127** and 65+3=**68**, and 50+127+68=**245**; the tree at `40b30aed` reads 127 / 68 over 245 rows. The error was mine, and it moved one row's worth instead of three, in both fields at once — which is the shape that survives a glance, because each field is a plausible neighbour of the old value | A count is checked by adding the row's statuses, not by rereading the one that changed |
| D113 | Whether D99's map bound covers a unit spec | **Answered: no — the bound names what can change what the app answers, not every file with `spec` in its name.** The map's 112 keys are what the running app answers (102 × “v4 answers 501 for …” + 10 `sql`), so the artefacts that can move one are the Playwright specs under `tests/`, the fixtures the app serves, `ci4-unported.json`, the comparator and `v4/src`. `v4/test/session/store.spec.ts` is a vitest unit spec and cannot move a key, so `v4/g1-map` stays at `68d3401c` with **no obligation from `40b30aed`**; the obligation stands, unchanged, for the first commit that touches one of those five | A bound written as a category collects readers who apply it to the letter and stop measuring |
| D114 | Whether a validation missing on one of three paths is a defect when the caller cannot reach it | **Answered: it is a divergence to rule on, and the ruling is the property, not the shape.** `loadSession` (`store.ts:33-36`) and `destroySession` (`:97-100`) refuse an id that fails `SESSION_ID`; `writeSession` (`:52`) hashes whatever it is handed and stores a row — so an id that nothing could load becomes a row. Unreachable today: the only producers are `newSessionId()` (43 chars of `[A-Za-z0-9,-]`) and a cookie id that has already passed `loadSession`'s gate before any write (`request.ts:155-166`). But the export takes any string, so the property is owed: **no row is stored for an id that cannot later be read, and the caller can tell** — shape is the builder's (a throw, or a result), pinned by one test that asserts a malformed id writes nothing **Corrected 02:17 EET 2026-11-26 — these are the numbers as measured at `36dba052`, not at the head:** at `f7dab7f0` the same gates read `loadSession` `:34-36` (returns `null`), `writeSession` `:64-66` (throws `TypeError`, before the digest and before any batch) and `destroySession` `:104-106` (returns silently — a delete of an id that never existed is a no-op by design, not an omission to “fix”) | An unreachable divergence is still a divergence, and the guard is cheaper than the argument about reachability |
| D115 | Whether the D1 bridge tests are ported | **Answered: by what each row asserts, not by the backend in its path.** Four rows are `dropped` under D109 with the replaced code cited — `D1DriverTest` 19 (v3’s query builder and the `d1.internal` bridge with its comment classifier and host allowlist), `D1HttpExecutorTest` 13 (the HTTP transport), `D1DriverBridgeTest` 6, `D1IntegrationTest` 3 — because v4 composes `statement()` SQL against `v4/src/db/sql.ts` and `D1Sql` binds in-process (`v4/src/db/d1.ts:12-37`). Four rows stay `scheduled` with their target moved out of S1.7 — `D1HostedWritesTest` 17 and `D1HostedBulkTest` 7 to P3, `D1HostedImportTest` 7 to P5, `D1HostedRefusalsTest` 2 to P7 — because their method names assert app behaviour (`…CreatedEditedAndDeletedAsOneBatchEach`, `…UndoOfADeletePutsTheItemBackOnce`, `…PruneDeletesExpiredTokensThenReleasesTheirFiles`, `…SearchIndexRebuildsAsOneBatch`) that v4 must have, and a drop would take the subject with the mechanism. **Re-measured 02:17 EET 2026-11-26:** the 17 method names split **9 app-behaviour, 6 unit-of-work/wiring** (`AUnitCannotOpenInsideATransaction…`, `AWriteBesideTheOpenUnit…`, `AnAtomicStepRunsOneUnit…`, `AD1ConnectionNeverGetsAConnectionExecutor`, `UnitsOfWorkOnAnotherD1ConnectionAreAWiringError`, `TwoSavesInOneAtomicStepAreRefusedBeforeTheSecond`) and **2 transitional** (`FeaturesNotConvertedYetAreRefusedBeforeAnyWrite`, `TheRefusalIsANoOpOffTheHostedDatabase`), so the 12/5 offered does not reproduce and P3 owes **9** subjects, not 12. And the `D1HttpExecutorTest` drop must cite `testRefuseBeforeTransportOnTooManyParams` by name, because that method’s subject is the one thing in those 13 that survives (D119). S1.7 → **22 rows / 153 methods**; ledger → **50 ported (346) / 123 scheduled / 72 dropped** over 245 rows | A row is a claim about a mechanism v4 replaced or about a subject v4 must build; the backend’s name in the path says neither |
| D116 | Whether a re-homing may name a file that does not exist | **Answered: no, and the first property it wanted re-homed was already asserted.** The proposal named `v4/test/db/d1.spec.ts`; the tree has no `v4/test/db/`, and the driver’s own unit spec is `v4/test/sqlite.spec.ts`, which already carries “leaves nothing behind when a statement in the unit fails, DDL included”. The second property — a guarded write under concurrency leaving one winner — has no counterpart: v4 has exactly one guarded write (`v4/src/db/migration-runner.ts:176`) and `grep -rn "ON CONFLICT" v4/test` returns no file, so it owes one v4-native test in the spec that exists: applied twice, one row, the second attempt reports no change | A port that duplicates a passing assertion adds a false count of evidence, and a path that does not resolve is a promise instead of a place |
| D117 | Whether a limit stated in a comment is enforced | **Answered: no, and this one is §10’s 100-parameter cap.** `v4/src/db/d1.ts:6` states it; `grep -rn "params.length" v4/src` returns no file, **and my sentence about the nearest test was wrong (corrected 02:17 EET 2026-11-26):** `v4/test/sqlite.spec.ts:66` is “binds 100 variables and refuses the 101st”, on the `DB_DRIVER=sqlite` driver, and its file header names the cap as D1’s 100 (§10 rule 3, “measured, not assumed”). So the cap is enforced by the engine on both drivers and by no v4 code; what the guard buys is an error v4 authors, before `prepare`, on the hosted driver where a 101-parameter statement otherwise fails inside a batch. The guard and its one test stand, scoped that way — the test asserts v4’s own error, never `/too many SQL variables/` So of the 41 methods D115 drops, one has a surviving subject as an absent guard: refuse more than 100 bound parameters in `D1Sql` before `prepare`, with one test. Same shape as D24 | A comment is a claim about code that does not read it |
| D118 | Whether a guard on an export nothing calls is the guard | **Answered: no — the live writer is `request.ts`’s own `insert()`.** `writeSession` and `touchSession` have no caller in `v4/src` (`grep` returns their definitions only), while `request.ts:348` writes the same row with a private copy of the same `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` at `:350`. D114’s property therefore protects the public export and not the path a request takes. The increment that next touches `request.ts` routes the live write through `writeSession`, or deletes the dead export: one statement, one writer | Two copies of one statement drift, and the gate ends up on the copy with no traffic |
| D119 | Whether a drop note must name the method whose subject survives | **Answered: yes, by name and by line.** `D1HttpExecutorTest`’s 13 methods are dropped whole, but `testRefuseBeforeTransportOnTooManyParams` (`D1HttpExecutorTest.php:26` at `5d65939e^`) asserts the very subject the D117 guard will cover — refusing before transport. A note that says only “the HTTP transport” leaves a reader to conclude the subject vanished with the mechanism, and the next reader re-opens a settled row. Every drop that re-homes a subject cites the method that carried it | A drop is a claim about the mechanism; the subject it carried has to stay visible, or the row gets re-argued |
| D120 | Whether a revision header may be rewritten as a line | **Answered: no — it is a prefix insert into one physical line that also carries revisions 84–103, verified by size and by marker count.** My first attempt at revision 104 replaced that line whole: **535,903 B → 485,995 B**, twenty revisions of history gone in a write that passed every other check, because the anchor was a line start. A header rewrite must leave the previous revision’s marker in place and raise the marker count by exactly one, and the byte count must be checked before the commit, not after | The plan’s history lives in the same line as its newest entry; a whole-line replace is a deletion wearing a rename |
| D121 | Whether a commit’s author address identifies a box or a script | **Answered: the script.** Every commit this box makes to the plan runs through a push script that passes `git -c user.email=…`, so `pm@webapp.gr`, `pm@apotheke.local`, `pm@apotheke.invalid`, `pm@example.invalid` and `pm@web.gr` record script generations, not writers — measured at `f7dab7f0`: 48 commits, 33 / 10 / 3 / 1 / 1, and `pm@web.gr` matches no script on disk. The builder’s box is `thordin9` only and never touches this file. One identity from now on; my “two writers will collide” is withdrawn | An author field is evidence about the tool that wrote the commit, and reading it as evidence about a person produced a false alarm |
| D122 | Whether a document’s structure can be trusted because its text reads correctly | **Answered: no — a table is a render, so it is rendered and counted.** Pandoc over §8 at `ec48c04e` yields **24 data rows of 119**: four blank lines end it (after D24 and between D118/D119, D119/D120, D120/D121), **40 rows** carry a pipe count other than 5, and one physical line holds three decisions — D39, D47 and D50. Repaired here: the blank lines removed, the line split three ways, D39 restored from `57d3baab` (713 characters there, 129 at the head, ending mid-word at “error an”), the D26 and D109 code-span pipes escaped, and the two lost tails marked in place. Rendered again: one table, 119 decision rows, four cells each. Still owed, counted: **36 rows have three cells**, consecutive from D53 to D89, and **D31 and D32 have no row at all** although §6 and §9 cite them | The lookup surface had been unrenderable since revision 63’s truncation, and D52’s repair was a size check, not a render |
| D123 | Whether one writer means one call | **Answered: no — it means one statement text.** D118 rules that the increment touching `request.ts` routes the live write through `writeSession`, and that cannot be followed as written: `request.ts` accumulates statements (`:109`) and sends them once (`:274-278`), so calling a function that batches would split the request’s atomic unit. The duplication is also **two** statements, not one — the `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` (`store.ts:72-75` / `request.ts:350-352`) and the touch `UPDATE` (`store.ts:93` / `request.ts:320`), which is why `writeSession` and `touchSession` are both dead. The fix is a pure statement builder in `store.ts`, carrying D114’s gate, that both the store functions and `request.ts`’s `insert()` use | One text, one gate, and the request keeps sending one batch |

### 8.1 D4 in plain terms (answered: G7 only)

G7 is the branch's own evidence: every suite green on `experimental-v4` — unit, schema parity on both engines, golden
pages, API contract, write replay, and the full Playwright E2E with untouched visual baselines. Stathis chose G7 as
sufficient: no dress rehearsal on a copy of real data, no shadow period. Two consequences we accept with that:

1. Quirks that only exist in real data (40 MP phone photos, HEIC uploads, old imports, odd encodings) surface after
   the switch rather than before it. Mitigation that stays inside this decision: the fixture set is widened during P5
   (restore of real-shaped backups), and the PHP image remains buildable from `main` as a rollback artifact without
   being deployed in parallel.
2. The switch is a single cutover on Stathis's word, so the merge proposal at G7 carries an explicit rollback recipe
   (re-point the Worker at the PHP container image and the pre-switch database/backup).

- 2026-11-23 12:50 EET — v4 scheduled as branch work, not a study: `experimental-v4`, `main` frozen, E2E + schema + backup parity as gates. Rationale: the study (`docs/future/workers-migration-plan.md`) already answered the architecture questions; the missing piece was an owner, a branch and gates.
- 2026-11-23 12:50 EET — DO SQLite kept as the app database; D1 stays control plane. Rationale: no data move, real transactions, in-process SQL. (Superseded 12:58: hosted app data moves to D1, one database per installation.)
- 2026-11-23 12:50 EET — v4 built in a new `v4/` workspace, folded into `edge/` in phase 6. Rationale: the branch must not be able to break `main`'s deploy job.
- 2026-11-23 12:50 EET — bcrypt retained (bcryptjs) instead of rehashing to PBKDF2. Rationale: no lockout risk, one code path, PHP keeps verifying v4 hashes during the dual-run window.
- 2026-11-23 12:52 EET — D1: PostgreSQL **not dropped**. Self-host keeps a real Postgres beside `workerd`; hosted stays DO SQLite. Rationale: removing Postgres would force every existing self-hoster onto an engine they did not choose, and the app is already dialect-abstracted. Cost accepted: +3 pw.
- 2026-11-23 12:52 EET — D2: server image work capped at 12 MP, browser pre-resize, client-side HEIC, originals untouched. Rationale: fits the Worker memory ceiling and removes the last Imagick dependency.
- 2026-11-23 12:52 EET — D3: HTML UI ported as is; SPA stays a separate track.
- 2026-11-23 12:52 EET — D5: one re-login per installation at cutover (same as 3.0).
- 2026-11-23 12:52 EET — D6: Workers Paid accepted for hosted; CPU cost re-checked at phase 4.
- 2026-11-23 12:52 EET — D7: merge/switch is a Stathis decision taken when the branch work is done; I report readiness, I do not merge.
- 2026-11-23 12:54 EET — D4: **G7 only**. No rehearsal on a copy of real data, no parallel run. Rationale accepted: the existing suites are broad (E2E + parity + write replay + backup round trip); the cost of the rehearsal was a week of calendar. Mitigation: real-shaped fixtures during P5 and a written rollback recipe in the G7 merge proposal.

- 2026-11-23 12:59 EET — **D8 answered: one D1 database per installation.** No shared database with an
  `installation_id` column. Rationale: it matches today's per-installation isolation, makes delete a single drop and
  backup/export a per-database operation, and keeps a failing installation from touching anyone else's data. Cost: the
  binding is resolved per request from the control plane instead of being declared in `wrangler.jsonc`, and
  databases-per-account ceilings become a real limit to watch (§10.4).
- 2026-11-23 12:59 EET — **No data move from current hosted installations.** They start on fresh D1 databases and
  re-import a backup if they hold data worth keeping. Rationale: hosted has been live since 2026-11-12, so the set is
  small and a copy path would cost more than it saves. Consequence: the switch has no unmigrated-data risk left, and
  the hosted cutover is a provisioning job, not a data job.
- 2026-11-23 13:25 EET — **Self-host packaging promoted to a deliverable (§11, S0.9), on Stathis's request.** Two compose files (`apotheke` + `rqlite`, and single-process SQLite), `.env.example`, a literal walkthrough in `docs/self-host-docker.md`, and `scripts/selfhost-smoke.sh` that CI runs against both. Rationale: a self-host promise that lives only in prose is a promise nobody tests; the compose file is also the cheapest way to pin env names, volumes and ports before the app hardens around them. Cost: about 1 pw inside P6, near zero for the phase-0 skeleton with a stub image.
- 2026-11-23 13:17 EET — **D9: no branch protection on `main` or `experimental-v4`.** Rationale: Stathis's call, and it removes a step that was twice reported as pending on him. What it costs: the `main-freeze` workflow only gates pull requests, so a direct push or a force-push to `main` is unguarded, and a mis-aimed push would publish PHP-replacing code with no review. Mitigation: both bots are instructed never to push to `main`, never to open a PR against `main`, and the PM reads `main`'s head before signing off any gate. Revisit if `main` ever moves without a release run.
- 2026-11-23 12:58 EET — **D1 revised again, on Stathis's clarification: hosted defaults to Cloudflare D1.** Self-host
  defaults to rqlite with plain SQLite supported; Postgres stays dropped. Rationale: D1 is the native hosted SQLite —
  no server to run, `batch()` atomicity, FTS5 already proven on that path in production, and provisioning/deletion map
  cleanly onto the existing lifecycle jobs. Consequences in §10: three backends, one dialect; the batch contract becomes
  the single unit-of-work model (which rqlite forces anyway); the `d1.internal` bridge and the DO-as-database-proxy
  become redundant on hosted. Net effort +1 pw against the original plan.
- 2026-11-23 12:56 EET — **D1 reversed: PostgreSQL is dropped.** v4 supports SQLite (DO) and rqlite; rqlite is the self-host default. No migration path is built — a v3 backup ZIP exported and imported into v4 is sufficient, for v3 SQLite and v3 Postgres installs alike. Rationale: rqlite is SQLite over HTTP, so the SQL text, the schema, the migrations and the parity comparison stay single-dialect; it needs no Node shims under workerd; and it gives self-hosters replication and snapshots without a second engine to test. What it costs: no transaction spans a request (audit in S0.4), one extra process in the self-host stack, and every query becomes an HTTP round trip. Supersedes the 12:52 entry that kept Postgres.

- 2026-11-24 14:05 EET — **Revision 27: the smoke is bounded at all three probes, and revision 26 could only name two.** Rationale: two of the three unbounded calls were `curl` and one was a raw `fetch` to rqlite, so a sweep that looked for `curl` found 2 of 3. The script's header now states the rule at `:97-101` so the next probe added is bounded by the text above it. What it costs: nothing — two one-flag commits, both green (`45382091338`, `45383878498`), and a red tree can no longer burn 37 minutes to learn nothing.
- 2026-11-24 14:10 EET — **D29: `sessioncsrf` becomes real inside 2a, at v3's 419.** Rationale: the filter's phase-0 403 made every users POST unreachable, so 2a could not be tested without it; and the status it refuses with is v3's own (`CsrfCheck::STATUS` 419), which the golden fixture and the E2E suite already expect, so the alternative was a green increment that diverged on every form write. What it costs: the stance changes for every surface at once — a token-less write is 419 rather than a plain refusal — and the change therefore has a number instead of living in a feature diff. Re-splitting was rejected: reverting a green commit to re-land 41 lines in two pieces costs a red window and buys only what this bullet records. Revision 28 also records 2a itself: `65d63014`, run `45390457762` green with 28 files and 248 tests, the page byte-identical to PHP on a pinned state.
- 2026-11-24 18:55 EET — **D31: the sqlite leg gets a job that names it, because the default driver was the most exercised and the least asserted.** Rationale: `wrangler.jsonc:49` makes `DB_DRIVER=sqlite` the default, so every existing test already ran the DO path in every job, and my dispatch premise — “only D1 and rqlite are proven, so sqlite is an assumption” — was backwards. What was actually missing: a spec naming the driver's contract, the count rule applied to that leg, and the DO's database read as bytes, while the `d1` job reads its own file and asserts the inverse. What it costs: one more job (~2 minutes) and `test/sqlite.spec.ts` now runs in every leg, which is the point. What it does not prove: a self-hoster's `node:sqlite` or system `sqlite3`, or a second process opening that file. Three facts measured here were new to both of us: the binding cap is D1's 100 (100 binds answer, the 101st refuses), a BLOB is an `ArrayBuffer` (rqlite `Uint8Array`, emulated D1 a byte array), and `PRAGMA`/`sqlite_version()` are refused on this driver and on D1 — that last pair measured by hand and documented in `adapters.md` at this commit, and **pinned by the sixth spec in `test/sqlite.spec.ts` from `74868d8f`** (revision 32).
